| `test_deploy_script_records_uptime_and_never_fails_on_it` | FR-15.2, FR-8.2: `deploy/student_endpoint.sh` start (creating the endpoint, since the fake `nebius` lists none) and stop (by the id the list gives) with `FakeNebius` record the endpoint in a real ledger through the CLI; with no ledger the deploy still exits 0 and says so; an unknown action exits 2. Skipped where bash is missing |
| `test_v04_database_gets_the_cost_columns_and_table` | FR-15.1, FR-15.2: a v0.4 file gains the split and token columns and `compute_intervals`; its old rows count as teacher spend (`split_unknown_requests`, a warning) and give no hosted-fallback price |
| `test_bad_cost_settings_stop_startup_naming_the_variable` | FR-15.3, NFR-11: a negative, non-numeric, NaN or 1e308 rate, fractional, negative or 4,096 GPUs, an unknown cost model and 25 endpoint hours a day each stop the runtime with the variable's name (9 cases) |
| `test_cost_settings_defaults_and_spellings` | NFR-11: the defaults, a cost model in any case with spaces, `RATCHET_PRICES_VERIFIED=1` changing the label, an empty model meaning `per_token`, and `--at` parsing |

### `tests/test_stats.py`

The exact statistics of `ratchet/stats.py` ([ADR 0024](adr/0024-evidence-foundations.md)). Monte Carlo checks are seeded, counts-only and sized to run in under a second; the two marked slow repeat them at full size.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_binomial_tails_match_brute_force` | `binom_sf`, `binom_cdf` and `binom_logpmf` equal sums of `math.comb` terms for every k of small n, edge probabilities 0 and 1 and 60 random cases at n = 120 |
| `test_binomial_tails_keep_tiny_values_and_refuse_bad_input` | 0.5^100 and 0.1^200 to relative 1e-12 and 1e-9 (summed directly, not as 1 minus almost 1); bad counts and probabilities raise |
| `test_clopper_pearson_bounds` | 46/48 → 0.8746, 129/130 → 0.9640, 127/128 → 0.9635; s/s → delta^(1/s); for 50 random (s, n) the bound is where P(X ≥ s) = delta, from below, and the upper bound mirrors it |
| `test_precision_pvalue_planner_and_mcnemar` | `required_rows`: 0.95 → 59, 93, 124, 153 rows for 0 to 3 errors; 0.97 → 99, 157, 208, 257; 0.90 → 29, 46, 61, 76; the p-value falls as agreement rises; McNemar 4 vs 0 is 0.0625 |
| `test_ltt_fixed_sequence_stops_at_the_first_failure` | 10 correct points certify nothing (1.01); 80 correct at 0.97 and 40 wrong at 0.72 certify 0.75 and stop at 0.70 with its trace; grid order does not matter; bad grids raise |
| `test_ltt_controls_the_error_rate` | Confidences U(0,1) with P(correct) = c: over 300 seeded draws of 400 rows, the chosen threshold has true precision below the 0.80 target in at most 5% (+3 SE) of draws |
| `test_e_value_oracles_and_confidence_sequence_oracles` | e(0/2 below 0.92) = 29.77, e(0/1) = 4.22, e(20/20) = 0.2006, e(7/17) = 2.59e5, e(60/60 above) = 27.6, e(83/83 above) = 129.7, within 1%; CS(0/2) = (0.001, 0.931) and CS(35/38) = (0.710, 0.992) within 0.002 |
| `test_alt_grid_components_are_supermartingales` | For p0 in 0.5, 0.8, 0.92, 0.97 and every true rate on the null side, each alternative's one-trial mean factor is at most 1 (the validity argument, checked exactly); weights sum to 1, also near the edges |
| `test_e_values_edges_and_input_checks` | No trials: e = 1; a huge count stays finite (capped at exp(690)); the confidence sequence narrows with data; bad sides, probabilities and steps raise; the cached confidence sequence equals a fresh scan and still refuses bad counts (a bool, a list, a float, s > n) |
| `test_e_process_false_rollback_rate_and_wilson_peeking` | At a true rate on the 0.92 floor, checked after every audit (120 runs of 200): the e-process rolls back at most 5% (+3 SE); Wilson's bound checked the same way does more than 10% (about 23%, simulation) |
| `test_confidence_sequence_covers_the_truth_at_every_step` | At p = 0.95, the true rate leaves the 95% confidence sequence at any step in at most 5% (+3 SE) of 120 runs |
| `test_e_process_and_confidence_sequence_error_rates_full` (slow) | The two checks above at 300 runs of 300 audits, at 0.92, 0.95 and 0.80 |
| `test_clopper_pearson_coverage_full` (slow) | P(cp_lower ≤ p) ≥ 0.95 (−3 SE) for (20, 0.90), (50, 0.95), (100, 0.97) and (130, 0.99), 2,000 draws each |
| `test_wilson_lives_in_stats_and_release_still_exports_it` | `release.wilson` is `stats.wilson`; 18/20 → (0.699, 0.972) |
| `test_kish_and_hajek` | Kish's n_eff of equal and unequal weights; the Hajek mean of a hand-computed example; empty or zero weights give None; bad input raises |
| `test_ratio_estimate` | R = 1.3 recovered from 400 synthetic pairs; the ratio and standard error equal the textbook formulas; equal weights change nothing; weighted case by hand; too few pairs or no x are `insufficient` (ratio 1.0, se None) |
| `test_ratio_estimate_interval_covers_the_truth` | R ± 1.96 se covers 1.3 in at least 90% of 200 seeded samples of 150 rows |
| `test_certificate_on_the_held_out_half_holds_at_its_rate` | FR-16.3: confidences U(0,1) with P(correct) = c, the threshold chosen on 200 calibration points by the point rule and certified at 0.80 on 200 fresh ones: over 300 seeded draws a certificate claims more than the truth in at most 5% (+3 SE), and some draws do certify |
| `test_stats_cli_plans_bounds_and_tests` | FR-16.3: `plan` prints `0 errors: 59 rows, 1: 93, 2: 124, 3: 153`, `bound` 0.9635 and 0.8746, `mcnemar` 0.0625 and 1; impossible counts and a target of 1.5 exit 2; `python -m ratchet.stats` in a subprocess |

### `tests/test_provenance.py`

Merkle trees and the BOM v2 scaffold ([ADR 0024](adr/0024-evidence-foundations.md)), and committed training sets ([ADR 0027](adr/0027-committed-training-sets.md)).

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_keyset_roots` | The empty set's root is 32 zero bytes, one key's root is its leaf hash, hex and bytes agree, and the root depends on the set, not the order |
| `test_keyset_proves_members_and_non_members` | 1,000 random members verify `included` (with their value) and 1,000 random non-members `absent` |
| `test_keyset_agrees_with_a_brute_force_set` | Sizes 0 to 40 over keys that share long prefixes: every key of a 120-key universe verifies `included` exactly when it is in the set |
| `test_tampered_proofs_never_prove_inclusion` | Flipping a byte of any sibling or of the terminal value makes a proof `invalid`; another key or an absent key never reads as `included`; an inclusion proof never reads as `absent` |
| `test_malformed_and_overlong_proofs_are_invalid` | A terminal leaf without the key's prefix, 257 siblings, missing fields, bad hex and wrong lengths are `invalid`, never an error; duplicate or short keys raise when building |
| `test_keyset_builds_and_proves_quickly` | 2,500 keys built and 200 proofs in well under 0.5 s |
| `test_log_tree_matches_rfc_vectors` | RFC 9162: the empty tree is sha256(''), the leaf hash of '' is 6e340b9c..., the Certificate Transparency 8-leaf vector's root is 5dc9da79..., and every prefix matches an independent implementation |
| `test_every_inclusion_path_verifies_and_alterations_fail` | Sizes 1 to 64: every audit path verifies (bytes and hex); an altered leaf or index, another tree's root, a size the path cannot fit or an extra node fails; malformed input is False |
| `test_bom_v2_manifest_sections` | Schema v2 with every v1 field, `redactor`, `training.origins`; commitments only when given (`ratchet.smt/v1`, size checked against the rows); `EXTRA_KEYS` merged; unknown sections, a wrong size and a bad root refused |
| `test_bom_verify_accepts_v1_and_v2_and_refuses_unknown_schemas` | Signed v2 and v1 documents verify with the public key, an unsigned v1 document by its hash; an unknown schema and junk are refused |
| `test_rows_report_their_origin` | `dataset.rows` reports `examples.origin`, or `live` when it is not recorded |
| `test_night_writes_a_v2_bom_with_its_redaction_setting` | `run_night(redact_pii=False)` writes a verifiable v2 BOM with `redactor.redact_pii` false and the origins of its 60 rows |
| `test_mock_gateway_bom_records_the_redaction_setting` | A mock `Runtime` with `redact_pii=False` writes it into its first adapter's BOM |
| `test_certificate_endpoint_and_stats_check_on_a_signed_bom` | FR-16.3, INV-14: on a mock gateway with a signing key, `GET /ratchet/versions/1/certificate` returns every key, the signed certificate, frontier and per-task block, the BOM's hash, the gate's evidence and three assumptions; 404 before training and for an unknown version; `drift` is the monitor's view of the version, with no alarm; a re-score changes `current` and the status, not `training`; a version from before v0.4 (no BOM) and v0.5 (no certificate) answers `no certificate`; `python -m ratchet.stats check` passes the BOM, fails it after `errors` is edited (hash and numbers), after a re-hash (numbers), without the key's signature, and passes a BOM from before v0.5 |
| `test_commitment_binds_each_examples_label_teacher_license_and_redactions` | FR-16.4: `dataset.commitment` keys are the example hashes in order; each value is the SHA-256 of a canonical JSON written out independently in the test (non-ASCII kept); a different label, teacher, license or redaction count moves the root; the order of rows does not; the empty set is `EMPTY`; a duplicate key raises |
| `test_version_leaves_are_written_once_and_found_by_key` | FR-16.4, INV-17: `set_version_leaves` writes and reads back in key order, an empty split writes nothing, `versions_containing` finds versions by key and split; a second write to a split, an unknown version, a duplicate key, uppercase or short hex, a null value and an unknown split are refused with nothing written; SQL UPDATE is refused by the trigger |
| `test_night_commits_the_exact_rows_it_trained_and_graded_on` | FR-16.4, INV-3, INV-14: two nights; each BOM's training and golden commitments equal `dataset.commitment` of exactly the rows the trainer and the evaluator got, size equal to `examples` and to the stored leaves; every golden key is `absent` from the signed training root; an example from night 1 is in both versions' training sets (cumulative retraining), one from night 2 only in the second |
| `test_a_failed_training_job_commits_nothing` | A training job that raises leaves no version and no leaves |
| `test_request_key_is_the_stored_hash` | FR-16.4, INV-12: for a plain request and one with a developer message, text parts, PII, `stop`, `response_format` and a temperature, with redaction on and off, `proof.request_key` and `resolve_key` give exactly the hash `Ratchet._store_example` stored |
| `test_resolve_key_validates_like_the_gateway` | A request without a task is `default`, normalised like the gateway's (developer role, text parts), `metadata.ratchet_task` and a body `task` (which wins); a key in any case; ten bad bodies refused with their reason; a part only the teacher reads refused because it is never stored |
| `test_proofs_and_the_held_out_certificate_through_the_gateway` | FR-16.4, INV-3, INV-6, INV-14: two mock days with a signing key: a v2 BOM whose commitments' sizes equal `examples`; the sample train example (prompt only) is `included` with its stored value and the signed root, size and BOM hash, the sample golden example `absent` from training and `included` in the golden set, by request or by key; both verify with `proof.verify_proof`; the default sample is the live version's (404 before there is one); the held-out certificate verifies with the exact success line; the CLI exits 0 for valid proofs and certificates and 1 for an unexpected result, another key's signature, a tampered sibling, a file that lies about its result, another root, BOM, version or split, an edited golden leaf, a missing proof and a file that is not JSON (2 for a missing file); 404 for an unknown version and `commitment not available` without a BOM, 400 for a bad split or body |
| `test_an_edited_leaf_fails_against_the_signed_root` | FR-16.4, INV-17: with the trigger dropped and one leaf edited, the proof and held-out endpoints answer 500 (twice) and write one `integrity` event; a proof built from the edited leaves verifies against its own root but fails against the BOM's (`root mismatch`, `invalid proof`) |
| `test_a_v1_bom_still_verifies_and_has_nothing_to_prove_against` | FR-16.4, INV-14: a signed v1 BOM verifies; `verify` and `heldout` exit 1 with `no set commitment`; `bom.commitment` is None for junk, another `alg` and a boolean size |
| `test_verifiers_never_raise_on_malformed_files` | FR-16.4: 14 malformed proofs (non-list siblings among them, the regression) and 11 malformed certificates each give `FAIL:` lines and no exception; a value in another hex case still verifies |
| `test_proof_endpoints_need_an_admin_key_in_live_mode` | FR-16.4, INV-8, INV-13: in live mode the proof and held-out endpoints are 401 without a key and 403 for an API key, admitted for an admin key (404: nothing trained), 403 `admin API disabled` without admin keys; the sample endpoint is 403 (mock-only) even for an admin |
| `test_cli_key_prints_the_stored_key` | FR-16.4: `python -m ratchet.proof key` prints `request_key` for a messages file (with `--no-redact` too) and for a request body with `metadata.ratchet_task` and `stop`; junk, a missing file, a string and empty messages exit 2 |

### `tests/test_privacy.py`

Data governance: storage opt-outs and pseudonymous end users (FR-13.1, FR-13.2, NFR-4, [ADR 0080](adr/0080-storage-opt-outs-and-pseudonymous-end-users.md)). Its invariants are INV-22 (opt-outs only reduce storage) and INV-23 (end-user identifiers are never stored or logged); their proving tests carry those markers.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_store_false_escalation_calls_the_teacher_and_stores_nothing` | FR-13.1, INV-2: `RequestMeta(storable=False)` on an escalation: the teacher answers, `Result.stored` false, `store_opt_out` `request`, the row `stored` 0 with its reason; the storable request stores, an opted-out duplicate (reason `key`) does not bump it; no `skip_example` event |
| `test_store_false_still_audits_and_arbitrates_without_storing` | FR-13.1, INV-15: with `audit_rate=1` the audit still records `audit_agree` 1 and nothing is stored; a shadow that disagrees is still arbitrated (`shadow_agree` 0) and the arbitration is not stored, while the same disagreement on a storable request stores it |
| `test_zero_retention_stores_nothing_for_any_caller` | FR-13.1, NFR-4: `store_examples=False` stores nothing for Scout's call shape (no meta), a request with an end user, or an audited student answer (still audited); every row `global`, `stored` 0 |
| `test_zero_retention_mode_night_skips` | FR-13.1: `run_night(store_examples=False)` skips with `storage disabled` before the trainer or the evaluator is called, although 60 stored examples would train; the default trains; the mock `Runtime`'s simulated day under `RATCHET_STORE_EXAMPLES=0` serves 100 requests, stores nothing and skips |
| `test_live_night_skips_under_zero_retention` | FR-13.1: `python -m ratchet.live night` with `RATCHET_STORE_EXAMPLES=0` prints `storage disabled`, runs no training command and creates no version |
| `test_no_store_tasks_from_the_setting_and_the_spec` | FR-13.1: `RATCHET_NO_STORE_TASKS` (spaces trimmed, unregistered names allowed) and a spec's `store: false` give reason `task`; other tasks are stored; a space-separated list or an over-long name stops startup |
| `test_examples_record_client` | FR-13.2, FR-3.3: an example keeps its first client and end user; a duplicate from another client adds one `example_clients` row, its own duplicates, internal calls and opted-out requests add none; an example first stored without a client records the first client that sends it again |
| `test_no_train_end_users_are_never_stored_even_when_registered_mid_request` | FR-13.2: a registered end user's escalation is served and not stored (`end_user`), another end user's is; a registration made while the request waits for the teacher still keeps it out (the store-time check) and is reported like one found at the start (`store_opt_out` `end_user` in the result and the row, counted in `not_stored_requests`), without changing the caller's `RequestMeta` |
| `test_pinned_and_teacher_only_requests_record_the_end_user_and_opt_out` | FR-13.1, INV-10: pinned and teacher-only rows record the end user's hash and the opt-out, and store nothing |
| `test_opt_outs_hold_under_concurrent_requests` | FR-13.1: 80 requests from 8 threads, three quarters opted out three ways (request, key, no-train end user): exactly the 24 storable ones are stored, and every row's `stored` matches its `store_opt_out` |
| `test_store_false_over_http_escalation` | FR-13.1: `store: false` over HTTP on an escalation: the student and the teacher are called once each, `ratchet.stored` false and `store_opt_out` `request`, nothing stored; `store: true` stores |
| `test_store_header_metadata_and_key_opt_outs` | FR-13.1: `X-Ratchet-Store` `none`, `0` and ` FALSE `, `metadata.ratchet_store` `"false"` and `"0"`, and a key with `store=0` in `RATCHET_KEY_LIMITS` (also with `store: true`, `X-Ratchet-Store: 1` and `ratchet_store: "true"`) store nothing; explicit opt-ins store; any one opt-out wins; an unknown header or metadata value is a 400 before anything is logged; `/ratchet/stats` names the key and counts by reason; an empty `X-Ratchet-Store` is the default (stored), not a 400 |
| `test_end_user_is_an_hmac_and_the_raw_value_is_stored_nowhere` | FR-13.2, INV-12, INV-13, NFR-4: with redaction off, `user` and `safety_identifier` (which wins) become 16-hex HMACs scoped per key in `requests` and `examples`; an empty `user` is none; a 309-character `user` is accepted and hashed whole (not as its first 128 characters); a non-string `safety_identifier` is a 400 that does not quote it; the raw values, a no-train value and the keys appear in no text value of any table; events carry no hash; `clients[].end_users` and `privacy.no_train_end_users` count |
| `test_end_user_hash_survives_reopening_the_file_ledger` | FR-13.2: the generated key survives reopening the file (same hash, no event); another file gives another hash; `RATCHET_END_USER_KEY` gives the same hash in any file; switching keys writes one `end_user_key` event without the key; a short key or a corrupted stored key raises |
| `test_after_no_train_registration_escalations_are_not_stored` | FR-13.2: `POST /v1/ratchet/no-train` with an API key registers the scoped hash without returning it (`created` true, then false); that client's end user is no longer stored, another client's same identifier is; 403 for another client, 200 for its own name; an admin registers once for every client (scope `*`), or for a named client (400 for an unknown one); a client cannot remove an admin's registration (`kept`), removes its own (`removed`, and storage resumes); an admin's removal for one client leaves the every-client registration (`kept`), its removal without a client clears it; `absent`; an admin registration over a client's own protects it; `no_train` events name who and never the end user or its hash; 401 without or with a wrong key |
| `test_no_train_requests_are_validated` | FR-13.2: missing, non-string and empty `end_user`, an unknown field (`unknown_parameter`), a non-string `client`, a non-object body and bad JSON are 400s in OpenAI's envelope; a 5,000-character `end_user` is accepted and never echoed; a keyless caller cannot name a client (403), registers in the keyless scope and gets `created` and `status` `null` either way; the mock cap (409) counts new registrations only, and a refused batch writes nothing |
| `test_a_no_train_registration_holds_whichever_identifier_a_request_sends` | FR-13.2: registered by `user`, a request that sends `user` and `safety_identifier` is still not stored; registered by `safety_identifier`, likewise; an admin's every-client registration by `user` holds for a request with both; another client's same values are stored; rows hold the `safety_identifier` hash and no alias |
| `test_no_train_registrations_across_key_renames_and_keys_added_later` | FR-13.2: a live file ledger across five startups: keyless registrations become unmatched once keys are on; an admin's every-client registration covers a key added later and a renamed one; a client's registration lapses when its key is renamed, and a `no_train_unmatched` event (names and counts only, once per change) and `privacy.no_train_unmatched` say so; the name back restores it; an admin's removal without a client reaches the unmatched keyless scope |
| `test_removing_a_registration_needs_a_key_in_live_mode` | FR-13.2, NFR-5: a live gateway without API keys registers for anyone but removes only for an admin key (403 otherwise) |
| `test_zero_retention_still_rescores_a_task_whose_spec_changed` | FR-13.1, FR-2.15: under `store_examples=False` the night skips (`storage disabled`, no training) but re-scores the live version on the stored golden rows of a task whose spec changed (new `spec_hashes`, a `rescored` event, no example written); nothing to re-score the next night; no re-score while a release is in flight |
| `test_every_add_example_caller_honours_storage_opt_outs` | FR-13.1: every function in INV-2's `ALLOWED_ADD_EXAMPLE_CALLERS` calls `store_opt_out` before `add_example`, so a writer added at a merge (a backfill) must honour opt-outs |
| `test_metadata_is_echoed_and_the_ratchet_block_says_whether_it_was_stored` | FR-13.1, FR-7.7: `metadata` echoed in the response, on the stream's `ratchet` chunk (the finish chunk, or the usage chunk with `include_usage`) and nowhere else, absent when not sent, never stored |
| `test_privacy_stats_report_opt_outs_and_clients` | FR-13.1, FR-13.2, FR-7.7: `/ratchet/stats` privacy keys on a fresh gateway (zero counts, `no_store_tasks` from the setting and specs, `no_store_clients`), `not_stored_by_reason` after traffic, `clients[].end_users` and `stored_share` (3 of 5), and `store_examples` false and `end_user_key` `env` when set |
| `test_key_limits_parse_and_refuse_typos` | FR-13.1, INV-13: `RATCHET_KEY_LIMITS` parsing (spaces, empty fields, several keys); an unknown name (not quoted, in case it is a key), an unknown field, a bad `store`, a key or field twice and malformed entries raise; with keys off every name is unknown |
| `test_storage_settings_refuse_typos` | FR-13.1, FR-13.2: `RATCHET_STORE_EXAMPLES` accepts 1/0, true/false, yes/no, on/off in any case (empty is the default) and refuses anything else; a non-bool in code stops `Ratchet`; a short `RATCHET_END_USER_KEY` and `RATCHET_KEY_LIMITS` without API keys stop the gateway |
| `test_ledger_from_before_opt_outs_gains_the_columns_and_tables` | FR-13.2: a file from before this change gains `requests.end_user` and `store_opt_out`, `examples.client` and `end_user`, `example_clients`, `no_train` and the `(end_user, ts)` index (used by the query planner), twice without change; old rows read NULL |

### `tests/test_erasure.py`

Retention, erasure and export (FR-13.3 to FR-13.5, FR-4.4, NFR-4, [ADR 0081](adr/0081-retention-tombstones-erasure-and-export.md)). Its invariants are INV-24 (an erased example is gone for good and leaves hashes only) and INV-25 (request retention never removes a release's evidence, also across the stages of a canary); their proving tests carry those markers. Time is injected (`now=`, aged `ts` and `last_seen_ts`, a `FakeClock` for the gateway's schedule).

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_erase_a_client_purges_its_examples_and_shared_ones_by_policy` | FR-13.3, NFR-4 (`delete`, `keep`): erasing `acme` purges its own examples and leaves globex's; under `delete` an example both sent is purged, under `keep` it stays with globex promoted to first sender (no end user) and acme unlinked, and a second erasure finds nothing; the `erasures` row and the `erase` event |
| `test_erasure_records_hold_hashes_and_no_text` | FR-13.3, NFR-4: an erasure by client, end user and text purges one example and unlinks one request row; no table holds the text, the raw end user or the email in the reason (stored as `[EMAIL]`); the scope holds the HMAC and the SHA-256 of `contains`; `erasure_items` and `tombstones` hold the hash; events hold neither hash |
| `test_dry_run_reports_and_writes_nothing` | FR-13.3: a dry run returns the counts and the affected version and changes no table or event |
| `test_affected_versions_are_reported_and_their_signed_boms_stay_unchanged` | FR-13.3, INV-14: two signed nights; erasing an early train row, an early golden row and a later train row reports v1 with 1 and v2 with 2 (`tainted`, status `pending`); both BOMs are unchanged and verify; a golden-only erasure reports none (`complete`) |
| `test_affected_versions_use_committed_training_sets_when_a_version_has_them` | FR-13.3: a version with `version_leaves` is counted by membership, one without by time; leaves win over the time rule |
| `test_bom_fingerprint_covers_kept_examples_and_counts_purged_ones` | FR-13.3, FR-3.5: `/ratchet/bom` changes its fingerprint (the SHA-256 over the kept hashes), counts `purged_examples` and leaves the purged row out of `sources`; `/ratchet/stats` privacy counts |
| `test_an_erased_hash_is_never_stored_again_and_a_retention_purged_one_is_restored` | FR-13.3, FR-13.4: `add_example` refuses an erased hash from another client (None; nothing bumped; `suppressed` 1); a duplicate of a retention-purged one restores the text with its own teacher and license, clears the purge and records the client; `dataset.rows` sees it again |
| `test_the_cascade_skips_a_tombstoned_answer_as_erased` | FR-13.3, INV-16: after an erasure the same request is answered by the teacher and logged `stored` 0 with a `skip_example` `erased` event |
| `test_erasure_makes_a_retention_tombstone_final` | FR-13.3: a retention tombstone keeps its end user, an erasure by that end user makes it final (reason `erasure`, end user cleared, purge time kept) and refused |
| `test_contains_matches_the_stored_text_literally` | FR-13.3: `%`, `_`, quotes and backslashes are literal (look-alike rows do not match); ASCII case folds, `É` does not; the answer and the conditions are searched too |
| `test_erase_refuses_a_bad_scope_without_quoting_it` | FR-13.3 (13 cases): no selector, bad hashes (format, a string, a non-string, none), blank `contains`, a bad client name, a blank reason or actor, an end user without the key, empty or not a string, a bad `shared`; never quotes the end user; nothing recorded |
| `test_erasure_holds_under_concurrent_stores` | FR-13.3: three threads storing duplicates while 150 examples are erased: no error, every row erased, none revived |
| `test_admin_erase_endpoint_needs_an_admin_key_and_is_a_dry_run_by_default` | FR-13.3: 401 without a key, 403 for an API key; a dry run by default; eight bad bodies are 400s that never quote the end user; an end user without a client is erased in every key's scope and the keyless one; events |
| `test_a_client_erases_its_own_end_users_examples` | FR-13.3: `DELETE /v1/ratchet/examples` erases the caller's end user's examples and unlinks its request rows, not another client's same identifier; 401 without a key; an `end_user` in the URL is a 400 that does not echo it; 403 for another client's scope; dry run; bad bodies; the actor and default reason; an admin erases across clients |
| `test_keyless_erasure_callers_learn_nothing_and_admins_reach_removed_keys` | FR-13.3: with API keys off, `DELETE /v1/ratchet/examples` answers a keyless caller with null counts for an identifier with data and one without (dry run and real); an admin's erasure of an end user for every client, by `POST` and by `DELETE`, reaches a key no longer configured |
| `test_retention_purges_examples_not_seen_within_the_window_golden_included` | FR-13.4, NFR-4: train and golden rows not seen for 30 days lose their text (reason `retention`), a recently seen duplicate keeps its row, one `purge` event; a second run purges nothing |
| `test_retention_purges_examples_a_chunk_per_transaction` | FR-13.4: with chunks of 2, seven expired examples are purged in four transactions, each under the ledger lock and the lock free before each; the rows, the result and the one `purge` event equal a single pass; a chunk of 0 is refused |
| `test_request_rollup_keeps_every_stat_identical` | FR-13.4 (chunks of 20,000 and 7): folding half the rows (a day split across the cut), then the rest, leaves `stats_by_day`, `stats_by_task`, `stats_by_client` (but `end_users`), `totals`, the opt-out counts and `cost_by_day` (also a day window) equal within 1e-9 |
| `test_fold_dry_run_counts_and_writes_nothing` | FR-13.4: a dry fold counts and writes nothing |
| `test_retention_never_folds_rows_of_a_release_in_flight` | FR-13.4, INV-25: a canary's rows older than the window stay (its audited precision unchanged) until it is promoted |
| `test_retention_keeps_every_stage_of_a_canary_for_the_anytime_valid_rule` | FR-13.4, FR-16.1, INV-25: a canary's rows from its 10% stage (two disagreements) stay after it moved to 50%, so `ReleaseGate.evidence` under the anytime-valid rule (12 audits, 10 agree; 4 in the stage) is the same before and after a fold; only the live version's older rows go, and the canary's go once it is promoted |
| `test_an_erased_committed_example_keeps_its_leaf_and_is_never_sampled` | FR-13.3, FR-16.4, INV-17, INV-24: erasing the example behind a version's first committed leaf leaves the leaves unchanged, and `Ledger.leaf_example` (the mock-mode gateway's sample) moves to the next leaf that still has its text, and to None once every one is purged |
| `test_day_numbers_survive_folding_the_rows_that_defined_day_one` | FR-13.4: a live file ledger keeps its day numbers and day 1's start after folding the oldest rows, and after folding all of them |
| `test_run_night_applies_retention_before_reading_the_ledger` | FR-13.4, FR-4.4: `run_night(retention=)` purges expired rows before the dataset is built: the trainer gets 60 of 70 |
| `test_the_purge_thread_never_touches_the_release_gates_lock` | FR-13.4: the purge thread, started while a tick holds the gate lock, leaves it held and raises nothing; a tick still takes and gives back its own (a merge once left `tick`'s `finally: release()` in `_purge`) |
| `test_live_gateway_purges_hourly_off_the_request_path` | FR-13.4: a live `Runtime` purges on its first tick on a background thread, not again within the hour, again after it; a failure is a `purge_failed` event with the class only; mock mode and zero windows never purge |
| `test_bad_retention_settings_stop_startup` | FR-13.4 (4 cases): a negative or too large window, a bool, or an unknown `RATCHET_ERASURE_SHARED` stop `Settings.retention()` and the `Runtime`; spaces and case are forgiven, empty is the default |
| `test_retention_days_must_be_whole_numbers_in_the_environment` | A non-number in `RATCHET_REQUEST_RETENTION_DAYS` stops startup naming it |
| `test_live_purge_cli` | FR-13.4: `python -m ratchet.live purge` with `--dry-run`, `--client`, `--older-than-days`, a bad window and no windows; a bad `--client` or a window past 36,500 days exits with a message, not a traceback |
| `test_run_night_trains_again_after_deletions_once_50_new_examples_arrive` | FR-4.4, FR-13.3, FR-13.4 (`erasure`, `retention`): after 60 of v1's 100 rows are purged, 49 new rows skip (`only 49 new examples`) and 50 train on 90 rows without the purged ones (v0.4's rule said -11); then three nights with no new data do not train (`only 0 new examples`; measured against v1's purges they retrained every night), a deletion of 10 of v2's rows is not new data either, and 50 new rows train on 130 |
| `test_a_purged_variant_still_counts_toward_its_requests_frequency` | INV-3, FR-13.4: a golden prompt seen three times over a bare and a stop variant trains; purging the stop variant keeps its prompt key and its count, so the bare variant stays in training |
| `test_export_round_trips_with_provenance_and_holds_no_keys` | FR-13.5, NFR-4, INV-13: every kind streams NDJSON as an attachment; examples equal the ledger's kept rows with their provenance and clients; requests have no end user; versions, erasures (items) and events; no API key, admin key, end-user key, end-user hash or raw end user in any line |
| `test_export_filters_and_refusals` | FR-13.5: `split`, `task`, `client` and `since` (ISO date, epoch, date-time) filters; 401 and 403; an unknown kind, a filter the kind does not take, a bad `since`, `split` or client name are 400s |
| `test_export_pages_through_large_tables` | FR-13.5: pages of 5 give all 23 rows in order, one line each |
| `test_admin_cli_erases_and_exports` | FR-13.3, FR-13.5: `python -m ratchet.admin erase` (a dry run, then `--apply` with the end user from stdin across every scope), its errors, and `export` to a file and to stdout, with a refused filter |
| `test_admin_cli_erases_end_users_only_with_the_gateways_key_and_never_writes_otherwise` | FR-13.3: `python -m ratchet.admin erase --end-user` in a shell without the gateway's `RATCHET_END_USER_KEY`, with another key or a short one is refused (dry run and `--apply`) and the ledger's meta, events and erasures stay unchanged; with the right key and no `RATCHET_API_KEYS` it finds the end user in every scope the ledger holds (a removed key's too) and logs no key change; a gateway's generated key is used from the ledger; a ledger that never hashed an end user is refused; a free-text `--actor` is refused |
| `test_a_ledger_from_before_retention_gains_its_columns_and_tables` | FR-13.3, FR-13.4: a file with the old `erasures` table gains the new columns and tables twice without change; `last_seen_ts` is backfilled from `ts`, and retention works on it |
| `test_an_erasure_during_the_champion_rescore_is_not_trained_on` | FR-13.3, INV-2, INV-17: an example erased while the live version is re-scored is not in the trainer's rows or the new version's leaves; the count and dataset hash match the rows; an `erasure_honoured` event, no `erasure_tainted` |
| `test_an_erasure_while_the_adapter_trains_taints_the_new_version` | FR-13.3, INV-14, INV-17: an erasure recorded inside `trainer.train` names no version when it runs; afterwards it is `pending` with the new version in `tainted`, one `erasure_tainted` event, the leaf stays |
| `test_an_erased_example_is_never_a_tombstone_hit_in_the_training_rows_of_a_quiet_night` | FR-13.3: an erasure before the night changes nothing the night writes (59 rows, no erasure event); `Ledger.tombstoned` answers by key |

### `tests/test_licensing.py`

Licenses that travel with the adapter and configured teachers (FR-3.50 to FR-3.53, NFR-7, NFR-9, INV-1, INV-14, [ADR 0090](adr/0090-licenses-travel-with-the-adapter.md)). It extends invariants 1 (configuration cannot relabel a known closed teacher) and 14 (the NOTICE is part of the signed BOM); the proving tests carry `INV-1` and `INV-14`. The live night runs through `live.main`'s seams (`run=`, `http=`, `provider_factory=`), offline.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_write_notices_lists_base_and_teacher_licenses` | FR-3.51, NFR-9, INV-2: `notices.sources` (URL, origin, distill_ok and count per teacher); NOTICE.md names the base model's license and URL, each teacher with its license, URL and count, "(configured)" for a configured one, teacher outputs only and the Ratchet version; the same bytes whatever the order; a warning for an unknown base model; no sources; cells that cannot break the table; `train.write_metadata` writes `ratchet_manifest.json` and NOTICE.md from the container's built-in registry |
| `test_base_model_licenses_cover_the_configured_base_models` | FR-3.50: the default student base model, the hosted fallback and the 30B checkpoints map to the NVIDIA Nemotron Open Model License, in any case; a local path, an unlisted id or a bare name do not; `base_license`'s warning |
| `test_bom_v2_has_base_model_license_and_v1_still_verifies` | FR-3.50, FR-3.51, FR-3.6, NFR-9, INV-14: `base_model_license`, each source's `license_origin`, `notices` with the exact text and its SHA-256; a warning, not an error, for an unknown base; the signature covers the notice; v1 and early v2 documents still verify and get a rendered NOTICE; `run_night` writes it |
| `test_live_night_puts_bom_and_notice_in_adapter_folder` | FR-3.51, FR-3.53, NFR-9, US-10, FR-3.6: `python -m ratchet.live night` syncs, trains, writes `data/adapters/student-v1/ratchet_bom.json` (the stored BOM's bytes, also in `data/boms/`) and the NOTICE.md the BOM signed, then syncs again (the second sync sees both files); it installs `RATCHET_TEACHER_LICENSES` itself, so the configured teacher is in the BOM and NOTICE; a night that does not train writes and syncs nothing |
| `test_live_night_reports_a_failed_second_sync` | FR-3.51: a failed second sync leaves the files written, writes a `sync_failed` event with the class only, prints the report and exits non-zero; without `RATCHET_SYNC_CMD` the files are written and nothing runs after training |
| `test_export_bundle` | FR-3.52, NFR-7, US-10, INV-14: `export` copies the adapter folder (nested files too), writes the verified BOM and the NOTICE it signed over the job's copy, prints the BOM's sha256 and returns a summary; `python -m ratchet.bom verify --notice` accepts the exported NOTICE.md and refuses an edited or missing one; refuses a non-empty folder or a file, a folder inside the adapter folder or anywhere in `RATCHET_DATA_DIR`, an unknown version, a version without a BOM, a tampered BOM, an adapter name outside the adapters folder, a missing `--version`, writing nothing; without local weights it writes the licenses and says where the weights are; `adapter_files` counts the adapter's own files, not the job's NOTICE.md; `python -m ratchet.live export` prints only the export's lines |
| `test_export_after_a_live_night_says_the_weights_are_in_the_bucket` | FR-3.52, FR-3.51, NFR-9, US-10: after a live night with an upload-only sync the adapter folder holds only the BOM and NOTICE.md `publish_bom` wrote; `export` (and `python -m ratchet.live export`) then counts no adapter files, reports no weights and says where in the bucket they are, never that it exported the adapter; a folder with a stray file but no weights still gets the hint; once `adapter_config.json` and the weights are there, it ships them |
| `test_config_teacher_license_allowed_and_closed_prefix_still_refused` | FR-3.53, INV-1, FR-3.1, FR-3.2: configured teachers are licensed with origin `config` and canonical license names; the built-in registry is a read-only copy source; seventeen closed-family ids claimed as Apache-2.0 are refused (`azure-gpt-4o`, `chatgpt-4o-latest`, `anthropic/next-model`, `openai/gpt-oss-120b`, `my_gemini_mirror`, `davinci-002`, `o5-mini`...) and so are router aliases (`openrouter/auto`, `.../auto:floor`), while open ids pass the family check and an unrecognised closed id (`x-ai/grok-4`) is shown to pass it too; five license names outside the allowlist are refused; a built-in teacher cannot be relabelled; a closed id forced into the registry still raises; after a reset the teacher is unknown again |
| `test_config_teacher_end_to_end` | FR-3.53, INV-1, FR-3.3: a configured teacher is refused without the file and licensed with it; its answer is stored with its license and URL; Night School trains on its rows and the BOM and NOTICE name it as configured; a cascade built without the file publishes the built-in registry, and the rows leave training |
| `test_bad_teacher_license_file_fails_at_startup` | FR-3.53, INV-1: bad JSON, NaN, not a list, too many entries, and twelve entry errors in one file, each listed with its place; a missing file; the registry left as it was; `Ratchet`, the gateway's `Runtime` in both modes, `python -m ratchet.live` and `python -m ratchet.admin` stop before opening the ledger; the fixed file starts |

### `tests/test_feedback.py`

Ground truth from people: request ids, verdicts and corrections, human labels as a provenance source, labelled-data import and golden-only rows (FR-13.30, FR-13.31, FR-3.54, FR-4.50, INV-1, INV-2, INV-3, INV-10, [ADR 0091](adr/0091-human-labels-as-a-provenance-source.md)). It amends invariants 1, 2, 3, 10 and 16 (`human` is never a teacher; human labels are targets, student outputs never; golden-only rows; no human label for a pinned task; the second writer of examples) and adds INV-26 (human verdicts never promote or roll back a version); the proving tests carry those markers. Gateway tests drive a live version whose student and teacher are `RecordingProvider`s.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_response_has_request_id` | FR-13.30: `ratchet.request_id` (`req_` + 32 hex) on every response, the completion id with the same digits, stored in `requests.request_id`; a stream's chunks carry it; internal calls have none; `parse_request_id` takes either form and nothing else |
| `test_feedback_only_on_own_requests` | FR-13.30, INV-13: another key's request is the same 404 as an unknown one, without the id quoted; 401 without a key; one row per request, a later verdict replacing it; no text or key in the table |
| `test_correct_verdict_never_creates_example` | FR-13.30, INV-2: a `correct` verdict on a student answer stores nothing and calls no model; a `correct` verdict carrying the student's text is a 400; task agreement in `/ratchet/stats` |
| `test_corrected_feedback_stored_with_human_provenance_redacted_and_verified` | FR-13.30, FR-3.54, INV-2, INV-3, INV-12: a correction stored as `human`, `customer-provided labels`, `verified`, origin `feedback`, with its client; messages and label redacted and counted; the teacher answer's hash and its split; Night School reads it |
| `test_human_overrides_teacher_on_same_hash` | FR-3.54, FR-13.30: a correction replaces the teacher's stored answer without counting the request twice; a later teacher answer only bumps `seen`; a JSON-mode request's correction needs its conditions to replace that variant, and other conditions are a 409 |
| `test_teacher_model_human_refused_at_startup` | FR-3.54, INV-1: `human` in any case as the teacher or fallback teacher stops `Ratchet`; never in the registry; a `RATCHET_TEACHER_LICENSES` entry named `Human` is refused; `lookup_source` |
| `test_feedback_body_is_checked` | FR-13.30: eleven bad bodies, each a 400 with its `param` and `code` (missing fields, a bad verdict or id, an unknown key, a non-string or invalid correction, messages without a correction or a correction without messages, an image part); nothing recorded |
| `test_correction_storage_rules_still_apply` | FR-13.30, FR-13.1, INV-10: the request's own opt-out, the key's `store=0`, a no-train end user registered after the request (the identifiers required and checked), a pinned task, `RATCHET_FEEDBACK_CORRECTIONS=0` and zero retention each keep the correction out with the reason, the verdict kept |
| `test_correction_needs_every_end_user_identifier` | FR-13.30, FR-13.2: a request that sent `user` and `safety_identifier` logs both keyed hashes; a correction with one of them is a 400 (`end_user_required`), a wrong second one `end_user_mismatch`; with both, a no-train registration made with `user` (the client's, or an admin's for every client) keeps it out; one identifier on the request needs one; the second hash is never exported and an erasure by either identifier clears both |
| `test_correction_conflicts_and_fixes` | FR-13.30: the same request's correction again replaces its own label (`seen` unchanged); other messages and a `correct` verdict after a stored correction are 409s |
| `test_label_precedence_human_teacher_import` | FR-3.54, FR-13.30, FR-13.31: teacher < correction < import; another client's correction never replaces one; a later import replaces an import; a teacher import never replaces a label; a retention-purged human label comes back as the teacher's; an erased example refuses a correction |
| `test_store_label_is_atomic_across_processes` | FR-3.54, FR-13.31: a second connection on the same file cannot write between `store_label`'s read and write (`Ledger.atomic`), so the import's label stands and the teacher's answer only bumps `seen` (from 0: the import was no occurrence); a refused golden-only row leaves no transaction open; an error rolls back |
| `test_human_precision_alerts_and_never_moves_a_release` | FR-13.30: one `human_alert` once `min_audits` verdicts give anytime-valid evidence that precision is below the floor, never again (meta row); the version stays live and the gate writes nothing; teacher-served answers count toward task agreement only |
| `test_human_alert_is_anytime_valid` | FR-13.30: the alert fires exactly when the mixture e-value against 'precision >= live_floor' reaches 1 / `ALERT_ALPHA`, checked after every verdict; 14 correct of 20 (Wilson upper bound under 0.92) does not alert |
| `test_feedback_outlives_folded_request_rows` | FR-13.30, FR-13.4: after request retention folds the row, the version's human precision and the task's agreement keep the verdict, and a new verdict on that request is a 404 |
| `test_concurrent_verdicts_keep_one_row_and_one_label` | FR-13.30: eight identical corrections at once: one feedback row, one example, `seen` 1 |
| `test_old_ledger_file_gains_feedback_schema` | an old file gains `request_id`, `end_user_secondary`, `verified`, `golden_only`, the `feedback` table and its indexes |
| `test_import_openai_finetune_jsonl` | FR-13.31, FR-3.54, INV-12: OpenAI rows, a multi-turn row with weights and content parts (earlier turns kept, address redacted), in-file duplicates and conflicts, six kinds of invalid rows with line numbers, tools hashing like a live request; imported rows start at `seen` 0; the `import` event (counts, attestation, SHA-256, no row text); importing again counts nothing twice |
| `test_import_refuses_closed_model_source` | FR-13.31, INV-1: twelve closed-family ids and a router alias refused with the clause quoted, also by the CLI before reading the file; unknown teachers, `teacher:human` and malformed sources; an open teacher's rows keep its license, unverified |
| `test_import_requires_attest_for_human` | FR-13.31: no, empty, multi-line or overlong attestation refused |
| `test_import_refuses_pinned_task` | FR-13.31, INV-10, FR-13.1: pinned, no-store (spec and setting), zero retention and unregistered tasks refused before anything is stored; rows of a task pinned later leave Night School; `store_human_example` refuses a pinned task |
| `test_golden_only_rows_never_trained` | FR-4.50, FR-13.31, INV-3, INV-6: golden-only rows whose hash says train stay golden, past FREQUENT and with every variant of the prompt; prompts already trained on are refused (`trained`); a real `run_night` trains on none and its BOM counts `golden.human_labels` |
| `test_labels_that_are_no_occurrence_never_reach_frequent` | FR-13.31, FR-13.30, INV-3: an imported variant of a twice-seen golden prompt (and a non-bumping correction) starts at `seen` 0 and leaves the prompt golden; the third real request makes it frequent; an imported prompt needs FREQUENT real requests; the export reports 0 |
| `test_golden_only_check_reads_the_ledger_at_each_row` | FR-4.50, FR-13.31, INV-3: a train-split row the gateway stores while an import runs keeps that golden-only row out (`trained`); the check is per row, in the transaction that writes it |
| `test_bom_lists_human_source` | FR-3.54, FR-13.31, INV-14: `sources` lists teachers only, `label_sources` the human labels by origin, `training.targets` and the NOTICE's Human labels section; an adapter without them gets the earlier NOTICE text; the ledger's `/ratchet/bom` lists them apart |
| `test_import_ratchet_train_jsonl_roundtrip` | FR-13.31: Ratchet's `train.jsonl` imports back with the same hashes; a teacher's rows cannot come in as human labels or under another task |
| `test_import_cli_dry_run_and_apply` | FR-13.31: `python -m ratchet.importer` dry run (refused for a ledger file that does not exist, which it does not create; an older file read without being migrated, byte for byte), apply (rows attributed to `--client`, one event), a missing file, a bad `--client`, a line over the body limit skipped |
| `test_counts_as_train_reads_human_labels` | a human training row counts toward the new-data count, a golden-only row never |
| `test_export_carries_label_flags` | the NDJSON export carries `verified`, `golden_only` and the provenance |
| `test_a_correction_a_policy_keeps_out_is_never_redacted` | FR-13.30, NFR-5: a correction under the request's opt-out or a key's `store=0` records its verdict without `prepare` running; a kept one prepares once; an answer the spec refuses is still a 400 whatever the policy |
| `test_import_refuses_a_client_whose_key_stores_nothing` | FR-13.31, FR-13.1: `--client globex` with `globex:store=0` is refused before any row is read (nothing stored, no event); another key and a name that is no key import; a limits spec the gateway would not start with is `cannot read the key limits` |

### `tests/test_receipts.py`

Verified savings receipts (FR-15.5, [ADR 0029](adr/0029-verified-savings-receipts.md)). Synthetic populations are seeded; simulation runs are a few days of mock traffic and assert trends.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_leaves_hold_only_metadata_and_the_key_name` | INV-12, INV-13: through a gateway with an API key, a request's leaf has exactly `LEAF_KEYS`, whole numbers and strings only, the key's name; neither the receipt, the leaves nor the proof holds the request's text, its answer or the key; no float anywhere in the manifest |
| `test_leaf_turns_amounts_into_whole_nano_usd` | `leaf` rounds USD to nano-USD (half to even), `label_p` to parts per billion and `ts` to milliseconds; NaN and missing amounts are null; `''` clients are null; `leaf_bytes` is canonical JSON |
| `test_ratio_estimator_recovers_the_ratio_and_its_lower_bound_covers` | y = 1.3 x (1 + 0.1 z) audited at 20%: the mean R over 200 seeded populations is within 0.01 of 1.3, and the one-sided 95% lower bound covers the true total in at least 180 of 200 (about 95% in 2,000) |
| `test_weights_follow_label_p_and_the_finite_population_correction` | Answers audited at two rates are weighted 1 / `label_p` (R equals the hand-computed Horvitz-Thompson ratio, n_eff below n); with every answer audited the variance is 0 and the point is the exact total |
| `test_insufficient_tasks_are_not_billed_and_the_fee_is_a_share_of_the_lower_bound` | A task under `min_audits` is `insufficient_audits` and only counts at its recorded baseline; the billable set is the teacher-served requests and the calibrated task's served answers; margin = ⌈1.645 se⌉; fee = ⌊share × net⌋ (share as a float or a string); spend outside requests comes off; a negative bound or no calibrated task bills 0 (`formula`); bad shares, minimums and spend raise |
| `test_stand_in_answers_are_never_billed` | `UNVERIFIED_ROUTES` equals the router's `STAND_IN_ROUTES`; a degraded answer and a fallback teacher's answer change neither the calibration nor the bill, and count at their recorded baseline |
| `test_unlogged_formula_estimates_are_never_calibrated` | Served answers without `baseline_est_usd` (before v0.5) make the task `unlogged`: no point, no bill |
| `test_integer_sums_are_exact_over_100k_rows` | 100,000 synthetic rows: the totals equal the sum of the rounded amounts exactly, within half a nano-USD per row of the float sum |
| `test_seal_is_idempotent_and_never_updates` | INV-17: a second `seal` and `seal_due` return the stored document and issue no INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE or REPLACE (`set_trace_callback`); `add_receipt` refuses an existing (period, client) |
| `test_period_rows_are_read_in_chunks_with_the_lock_free_between_them` | `period_rows` reads `RECEIPT_CHUNK` rows per locked statement: another thread logs a request between chunks; the chunks equal the one read, in id order, a client's rows only for a client; `receipt_row` reads one row |
| `test_concurrent_sealing_writes_each_receipt_once` | 8 threads sealing at once write each (day, client) receipt once with no error; a second connection to the same file that loses the race keeps the first writer's document |
| `test_a_day_is_sealed_only_once_it_is_over` | The current day raises `PeriodOpen`; a day without requests has no receipt; on a live ledger a day is closed 5 minutes after it ended, not before |
| `test_signature_verifies_and_edits_break_it_and_the_chain` | Three chained days: `prev_sha256` links each to the one before; the signature verifies with the public key and fails with another; an edited total fails, also re-hashed; an earlier receipt with a lowered fee, or the wrong day, breaks the chain check |
| `test_receipts_are_chained_per_client_and_hold_only_that_clients_ids` | Two clients and anonymous traffic over two days: each client's receipt holds only its ids, rechecks from its own leaves and chains to its own previous receipt; the gateway's covers every request; GPU-view notes |
| `test_spend_outside_requests_is_netted_and_shared_by_request_count` | A backfill spend row comes off the gateway's bound in full and off a client's by its share of the day's requests |
| `test_recheck_recomputes_everything_from_the_leaves` | `recheck` passes on the stored leaves; a changed amount (root and totals), reversed order, a missing leaf and a fee beyond the tolerance are caught; an estimated amount within 2 nano-USD is not |
| `test_verifiers_never_raise_on_malformed_input` | 8 junk documents: `verify_receipt`, `verify_chain`, `verify_proof`, `verify_rows` and `check_manifest` return failures, never an exception |
| `test_gateway_returns_the_request_id_and_its_proof_verifies` | A signed mock gateway: `X-Ratchet-Request-Id` and `ratchet.row_id` (also on the last stream chunk; `ratchet.request_id` is the feedback id); the day is 409 until it ends, then sealed by the live gateway day; the listing, the signature, the GPU view; each request's proof verifies with `merkle.verify_inclusion` and `verify_proof`; unknown ids 404, bad periods 400; the leaves recheck; the next day chains to it |
| `test_api_keys_read_only_their_own_receipts` | With API and admin keys: client B gets 403 on client A's receipt, proof and leaves, 404 for A's request in its own receipt; no key or a wrong key 401; an admin reads the gateway's and every client's receipt, and the listing per caller |
| `test_live_mode_without_keys_serves_no_receipts` | Live mode without keys: 403; the mock-mode gateway without keys is open |
| `test_rows_changed_after_sealing_are_caught` | A row slipped into a sealed day: proof and leaves answer 500 (twice) with one `integrity` event, and the receipt is unchanged; a row edited after its tree was cached is caught too (the proof reads its row again) |
| `test_request_retention_never_breaks_a_receipt` | FR-15.5, FR-13.4: a signed mock gateway; day 0 is sealed and a proof served (its tree cached); a fold takes day 0's rows and one of day 1's: day 0's receipt is unchanged and verifies, its proof (from the cache too) and leaves answer 410 `folded by request retention`, never the integrity 500; day 1, folded before it was sealed, is never sealed from the row left (410, one `receipt_skipped` event however often it is asked for) and day 2 chains to day 0 |
| `test_live_purge_seals_every_ended_day_before_it_folds` | FR-15.5, FR-13.4: `python -m ratchet.live purge` with a 2-day request window on a live ledger seals days 1 and 2 (the client's and the gateway's receipts, 2 and 1 leaves) before it folds their 3 rows; no `receipt_skipped` |
| `test_bad_receipt_settings_stop_the_gateway` | 5 cases: a take rate above 1 or NaN, a minimum of 1 audit, a negative or huge mock reasoning mean stop the gateway with a message naming the variable |
| `test_receipt_settings_are_read_and_checked` | Defaults 0.25 and 30 and no mock mean; the variables read and no longer reserved; `share_text` |
| `test_mock_teacher_reasoning_varies_around_its_mean_deterministically` | The default bills exactly 300; with mean 420 and cv 0.3 the same request always costs the same, the mean is 395 to 445 over 400 requests, `out_tokens` follow; cv 0 bills the mean |
| `test_sealing_moves_no_simulation_number` | `--receipts` on, off and with `seal_due` monkeypatched out: identical days, totals, versions, nights, events and serving; with the formula right (300 tokens) the bound held and R is about 1 |
| `test_receipts_measure_and_correct_a_teacher_that_reasons_more` | `--teacher-reasoning-mean 420` (simulation): each calibrated day's `ticket-triage` R is above 1.2, the formula's baseline is below 95% of the true cost and the calibrated one within 10% of it, a fee is billed on the bound, and the bound is at or below the truth on all but one day at most |
| `test_cli_verifies_a_bundle_and_fails_after_a_one_digit_edit` | `python -m ratchet.receipt verify` with the key, the previous receipt, a proof and the leaves: four OK lines, exit 0; one digit of the fee changed: exit 1 (`sha256 does not match`); not JSON: 1; a missing file: 2 |
| `test_cli_module_entry_point` | The same as a subprocess (`python -m ratchet.receipt`), with an unchecked chain reported |
| `test_offline_seal_of_a_simulated_ledger` | `python -m ratchet.receipt seal --db` on a `sim.simulate --db` ledger: every logged day sealed and labelled simulation, a day after the run 2, nothing left to seal 0, a missing file and `--client` without `--period` 2 |
| `test_live_seal_command_seals_every_day_that_ended` | `python -m ratchet.live seal` on a live ledger seals every client's and the gateway's receipts of the days that ended (not today's), then says nothing is left; labelled live traffic |
| `test_the_gateways_purge_never_folds_a_day_that_has_not_been_sealed` | FR-13.4, FR-15.5, INV-17: `Runtime._purge` with a day that ended 100 s ago (inside the 300 s grace) folds the earlier day and leaves the ended one whole; an hour later it is sealed from both of its rows, no `receipt_skipped` |

### `tests/test_docs_claims.py`

The public claims (NFR-70). A judge who knows the field reads the README, the PRD and the competitive analysis; these tests keep the overclaims out and the prior art in. They read files only.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_no_overclaims` | NFR-70: the 11 files in `CLAIM_FILES` (the README, `docs/README.md`, PRD, competitive analysis, business plan, glossary, architecture doc, the lite landing and dashboard pages, `web/index.html` and `pyproject.toml`), the lite pages (`ratchet/web/*.html`, `ratchet/dashboard.html`), `web/index.html`, `pyproject.toml` and every `web/src` `.ts` and `.tsx` file contain none of the overclaims: owning the model or its weights ("own the model weights", "own the weights", "weights you keep", "model you own", "model that you own", ...), "Your bill only goes down", "No competitor sells license-clean distillation", "automates all six" or "runs all six", a stopped endpoint that "costs nothing", "Llama-only" and "One student founder built it", in any case, split over lines, inside `**` or `<strong>`, with `&nbsp;` or a JSX `{" "}` spacer (the matcher is checked on such samples first, among them the old React hero's "weights you keep"); a competitor's own words quoted as theirs (Experiential's "a specialized model you own") are allowed only where listed, and only while the file still quotes them; every listed file exists and the glob finds the React pages |
| `test_unsourced_competitor_claims_are_gone` | NFR-70: Experiential Labs' "at least 50% lower cost with an SLA" and "Orchestra routes per task", for which no source was found, and "a person approves" for Inference.net, whose launch post only says new models are "staged for final review and deployment", are in none of the README, competitive analysis or business plan |
| `test_readme_names_prior_art_and_limits` | NFR-70: the README has "NVIDIA lineage" (the six steps of arXiv 2506.02153 v3 in the paper's words and order, each mapped to Ratchet, the paper's link and the Data Flywheel Blueprint's human promotion), "Prior art and what is new" (the gateways, distillation services such as OpenPipe and Inference.net, cascades, online distillation, OpenRouter's `enforce_distillable_text`, NVIDIA's tutorial, Trust or Escalate and Netflix's sequential canary tests, with at least 14 links, and what Ratchet adds) and "Limitations" (break-even, the hosted student without `RATCHET_STUDENT_BASE`, agreement rather than ground truth, the pattern-based redactor, Scout's unchecked `key_fact`, mock models, ToS §5(f)); "Why license-clean" quotes OpenMDW-1.1's outputs clause verbatim with its link |
| `test_nvidia_lineage_is_stated_exactly` | NFR-70: the lineage table has an "Automatic?" column: Yes for S1, S2, S5 and S6, "No: manual" for S3 (tasks are named, not clustered) and S4 (one base model), and the paragraph above it says exactly that; the Data Flywheel Blueprint's "remains a human decision" and "a flashlight, not an autopilot" and the Nano 4B model card's Nemotron Elastic sentence are quoted verbatim with their dates; the competitive analysis says four of the six steps, not six |
| `test_readme_compares_routers_and_distillation_services` | NFR-70: "Why not a router or a distillation API?" compares a cascade router, the distillation services (OpenPipe, AWS Bedrock, OpenAI) and Ratchet on who owns the student, the teacher license per example, the nightly retrain, the statistical release gate and reporting when it loses money, and names the YC teams; Inference.net and OpenAI's deprecation are quoted in their sources' words; "How the repo was built" dates the history (Oct 19, 2026), names the generated files and claims no sole authorship |
| `test_ownership_wording_on_every_first_screen` | NFR-70: `pyproject.toml`'s description, `docs/README.md`, `web/index.html`, the React hero and the lite landing page's meta description and hero say "a Nemotron adapter you own (LoRA on NVIDIA's open Nano base)"; the hero diagram's Nano node reads "adapter you keep" |
| `test_nebius_facts_are_worded_as_documented` | NFR-70: no README, doc (this file, which names the phrases, aside) or deploy file says a stopped endpoint costs nothing (Nebius bills a mounted bucket while it is stopped) or that Token Factory's LoRA serving is Llama-only; the PRD's dependency row says what a stopped endpoint is billed for |
| `test_not_built_yet_matches_the_prd` | NFR-70: a README "Not built yet" line names its PRD row, and no row it names (by id, or by topic: committed training sets and Merkle proofs are FR-16.4) is Done; the checker itself flags FR-16.4 on a copy of the PRD where it is Done, so the merge that lands committed training sets has to update the README |
| `test_ownership_is_stated_exactly` | NFR-70: one README line says the customer owns the LoRA adapter and its signed training lineage, names the base's license and warns that a GPU can cost more than it saves; the lite landing page's closing line is that sentence; the PRD's overview, G5, P3 and US-10 say "adapter" |
| `test_competitive_analysis_cites_sources_and_dates` | NFR-70: the competitive analysis has an as-of date; its summary's first paragraph ends with the narrower claim (OpenRouter filters at request time; no gateway found records and signs each example's lineage); the OpenRouter, Data Flywheel, NVIDIA tutorial, TensorZero, Inference.net and Portkey rows each link their source and give a date |

### `tests/test_perf.py`

The hot path ([ADR 0060](adr/0060-hot-path-caches-and-throttled-tick.md), FR-12.8, NFR-1). See [Hot-path tests](#hot-path-tests) for how the timing tests stay stable.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_file_ledger_uses_wal_and_memory_does_not` | A file ledger: WAL, `synchronous=NORMAL`, 5 s busy timeout, a read-only second connection that sees each commit at once and refuses writes; `:memory:` has none; an old rollback-journal file is switched on open and gains `wall_ms` |
| `test_sync_full_keeps_the_rollback_journal_and_bad_values_stop_startup` | `RATCHET_DB_SYNC=FULL` (any case) takes a WAL file back to the rollback journal with `synchronous=FULL` and no second connection; anything else raises naming the variable; `config.SYNC_MODES` matches the ledger's |
| `test_full_cannot_take_a_file_out_of_wal_while_another_process_holds_it` | `FULL` on a file another connection holds in WAL stops with an error naming the setting; `NORMAL` alongside is fine |
| `test_gate_queries_search_their_indexes` | `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` of the ledger's own query constants: the audit tally searches `requests_version_audited` from the last id, the shadow verdicts `requests_shadow_version`, the routing rebuild `versions_state`, a day's percentiles `requests_day`; none scans `requests` |
| `test_version_cache_sees_another_connections_writes` | Two ledgers on one file (gateway and cron tick): a new live version, canary steps, a rollback and a new eval written by one are seen by the other on its next read |
| `test_version_cache_invalidates_on_state_change` | Every version write (create, eval, state, traffic, BOM, and SQL on `ledger.db` that bypasses the writers) is seen on the next read; each read is a fresh copy, so changing one changes nothing cached; `version(id)` agrees |
| `test_routing_reads_no_version_rows_until_a_version_changes` | With 31 versions, ten routing rounds run only `PRAGMA data_version`; the next version write makes the next read rebuild once |
| `test_live_quality_tally_matches_a_full_count` | `version_live_quality` equals one `COUNT(audit_agree)`/`AVG` over the stage after new audits (read above the last id), audits without a verdict (pending off-path audits, counted in neither number; ADR 0072), other versions' and teacher rows, a new stage, a deleted row, and rows another connection commits |
| `test_bom_digest_is_cached_until_examples_change` | The BOM digest is recomputed after an insert, a rewritten or deleted hash, or another connection's insert, not after a duplicate; never cached against an uncommitted write; always equal to a fresh computation |
| `test_events_last_reads_only_the_newest` | `events(last=n)` is `events()[-n:]`; 0 gives none; a bad `n` raises |
| `test_stats_reads_skip_the_writer_lock` | On a WAL file every stats query returns while another thread holds the writer's lock |
| `test_reader_methods_use_only_the_reader_connection` | FR-12.8, FR-12.31: no `@_reads` method touches `self.db` (the writer's connection, which a reader holds no lock for; `last_ids` did) |
| `test_reads_and_an_offpath_writer_never_deadlock` | FR-12.8, FR-12.31: in a child process (the freeze takes the interpreter with it), every `_reads` method that needs no arguments against a thread running `update_request` and logging requests on a WAL file for a second; a deadlock dumps every stack after 30 s and fails the test |
| `test_tick_throttle_runs_the_gate_once_per_interval` | With a 5 s interval on a fake clock: one gate tick per interval, none while another thread ticks (without waiting), `force` and simulated days always tick, a failing tick is not retried on every request, interval 0 ticks every call |
| `test_request_path_ticks_at_most_once_per_interval` | Through `/v1/chat/completions`: three requests within the interval share one tick; the next one after it ticks |
| `test_snapshot_cache_ttl_and_single_flight` | `SnapshotCache`: one value per TTL; during a recompute other callers get the previous value at once, and the very first callers wait; a failed recompute raises and keeps the old value; TTL 0 computes every time |
| `test_stats_ttl_returns_the_cached_payload` | `/ratchet/stats` with a 2 s TTL returns the same payload within it despite a request, the new one after it, and a fresh one after a test run reset |
| `test_interval_settings_have_mode_defaults_and_bounds` | 5 s and 2 s in live mode, 0 in mock; 0 to 3600 s; negative, too long, NaN, infinite, bool and unparsable values raise naming the variable, also at gateway startup |
| `test_latency_percentiles` | Nearest-rank p50 and p95 of `wall_ms`, exact, by day and `served_by`; q 0 and 1 are the minimum and maximum, 0.07 of 20 is rank 2; old rows without `wall_ms` count toward nothing; None without rows; bad quantiles raise |
| `test_stats_by_day_reports_latency_percentiles_and_sorts_only_changed_days` | `stats_by_day`'s `p50_student_ms` and `p95_ms` equal `latency_percentiles`; a second read sorts nothing, a new request sorts only its day, a deleted row every day |
| `test_wall_ms_logged_and_at_least_latency` | Over `OpenAICompatProvider` and a fake server, cold start, student-served, escalated, pinned and teacher-only rows each have `wall_ms >= latency_ms > 0` |
| `test_wall_ms_is_the_timer_around_the_whole_cascade` | With `Ratchet(timer=...)`, `wall_ms` is exactly the two timer reads apart, on `handle`, a pinned task and `teacher_only`; `latency_ms` is the provider's |
| `test_handle_overhead_does_not_grow_with_versions` | Zero-latency models: the mean request time with 365 retired versions is at most twice the one-version mean plus 0.5 ms (v0.4's lookups took about 25 times as long) |
| `test_perf_guard_200k_requests` | A WAL ledger with 200,000 requests and a live, canary and shadow version: a cold tick under 250 ms, a warm tick under 20 ms (median), a request under 5 ms of its own time, the tally still exact |

### `tests/test_security.py`

Endpoint access, scrubbed public stats, the OpenAPI switch, the live-mode auth guard and security headers ([ADR 0061](adr/0061-endpoint-access-levels.md), FR-12.7, NFR-5, INV-13). A private live gateway runs offline through `Runtime(transports=)` with three kinds of keys.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_every_route_has_an_access_level` | Every route the app serves has a level in `access.ROUTES` or `access.PREFIXES`, and the Access column of docs/API.md shows the same one; HEAD reads as GET, the prefixes cover routes other packages add (`/ratchet/requests/...`, `/v1/ratchet/no-train`), `/livez`, `/readyz`, `/metrics` and `/ratchet/kpis` are pre-classified, and an unknown route is not guessed |
| `test_private_live_dashboard_needs_an_admin_or_dashboard_key` | Every dashboard and BOM route of a private live gateway: 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` without a key or with a wrong one, 403 for an API key, 200 for an admin or dashboard key (Bearer or `X-API-Key`); health, the BOM public key, the pages and Scout stay open; a dashboard key cannot write tasks (403) or call `/v1/*` (401); keyed readers see client names and no key reaches the payload or the database; `RATCHET_PUBLIC_BOM=1` opens only the BOM endpoints |
| `test_private_dashboard_without_reader_keys_is_closed_to_everyone` | No admin or dashboard keys: every read is 403 naming `RATCHET_DASHBOARD_KEYS`, also with an API key |
| `test_mock_dashboard_is_public_by_default` | Mock mode: the dashboard API is open with or without admin and API keys, and unscrubbed; `RATCHET_PUBLIC_DASHBOARD=0` closes it like a live one |
| `test_public_live_stats_are_scrubbed_for_anonymous_readers` | A public live dashboard: anonymous readers get `client-1`, `client-2` by sorted name (the same pseudonyms in task allow-lists), a v0.4 `ConnectError: ...` event cut to the class, URLs as `<url>`, no client name, address or key; admin and dashboard readers get the full payload from the same cached one (never changed by a scrubbed copy); `RATCHET_SCRUB_STATS` 1 and 0 |
| `test_scrub_helpers` | `scrub_detail` on v0.4 and current event formats (today's class-and-status details are kept), `pseudonyms`, `scrub_stats` leaving its input unchanged, `scrub_task` |
| `test_live_mode_without_api_keys_does_not_start` | A live `Runtime` without API keys raises before the ledger file exists; `allow_unauthenticated` lets it start; mock needs no keys; the same from the environment as uvicorn loads it |
| `test_each_key_has_one_role` | A dashboard key that is an API or admin key, a short one, or an admin key that is an API key stops startup naming the variable |
| `test_access_switches_resolve_by_mode_and_refuse_typos` | Mock and live defaults; explicit 0 and 1; set but empty is the default; `yes`, `true`, `2`, `on` stop startup naming the variable; a CSP with a line break is refused; `Access.scrubs` for every case |
| `test_openapi_is_off_in_live_mode` | Live: `/openapi.json`, `/docs` and `/redoc` are 404, back with `RATCHET_OPENAPI=1`; mock serves the schema, `0` removes it |
| `test_security_headers_on_every_response` | `nosniff` and `no-referrer` on a page, health, stats, a chat completion, a stream, a 400, a 404, a 413 from the body limit, Scout, and both 500s (sent outside the middleware); no CSP by default; `RATCHET_CSP` adds it |
| `test_unauthenticated_live_mode_needs_the_edge_guard_it_relies_on` | NFR-5, FR-12.7, FR-12.30: `allow_unauthenticated` alone, with the guard open, a missing token, a 31-character token or a misspelled guard is refused at startup (before the ledger opens) and by the config check, naming both variables and never the token; ` Guarded ` and 32 characters start; API keys need no guard |

### `tests/test_observability.py`

Request ids, the failures table, structured logs, Prometheus metrics, trace forwarding and version-transition events ([ADR 0062](adr/0062-request-ids-failures-logs-and-metrics.md), FR-1.60, FR-12.4, NFR-6, NFR-3, INV-12, INV-13). Gateways run in mock mode or as private live gateways through `Runtime(transports=)` with `FakeOpenAIServer` and `httpx.MockTransport` teachers; the cascade runs on `RecordingProvider`s. `parse_metrics` is a small parser of the text format 0.0.4 that fails on any malformed line or duplicate series.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_request_id_echoed_and_client_id_stored` | `x-request-id` (`req_` + 32 hex) and `openai-processing-ms` on a completion; `id` is `chatcmpl-` + the same digits and `ratchet.request_id` the id; a valid `X-Request-Id` or `X-Client-Request-Id` lands in `requests.client_request_id`; spaces, 129 characters, `;` and non-ASCII bytes are ignored without an error; every page and 404 gets its own id, never the client's |
| `test_streamed_answer_carries_the_request_id` | A stream's header, every chunk's `id` and the last chunk's `ratchet.request_id` |
| `test_error_responses_carry_x_request_id_and_envelope_request_id` | A 400, 401, 413 and 500 carry the header and `request_id` beside OpenAI's untouched four-field `error`; the 400 writes no failures row, the 401 an `auth` row (`HTTPException 401 invalid_api_key`, no client), the 413 a `gateway` row, the 500 a `gateway` row naming the class only; a non-`/v1` 404 keeps `{"detail"}` with the header |
| `test_request_context_parsing` | `new_context`: a padded client id, a valid `traceparent` and `tracestate`; the second header when the first is invalid; upper case, all-zero ids, version `ff`, extra fields on version `00` and a short parent id refused; a later version's extra fields accepted; a 600-character `tracestate` dropped; `lookup_hex`; no headers outside a request |
| `test_traceparent_forwarded_to_the_teacher_with_a_new_span` | Each teacher attempt (a 503, then the retry) carries the trace id, flags and `tracestate` with its own new span id, never the client's; a malformed or missing `traceparent` sends none |
| `test_request_lookup_own_request_and_another_clients` | `GET /v1/ratchet/requests/{id}` by either id form: the own request's metadata (no text); another key's request and an unknown id are the same 404 `request_not_found`; malformed ids 404; no key 401; a quota 429 is found by its failures row alone; `GET /ratchet/requests/{id}` reads any request |
| `test_teacher_failure_row_carries_the_student_cost` | An escalation whose teacher fails: no requests row, one `teacher` row with the student's sample cost, status 502, the client, task, both ids and `UpstreamError 502 teacher_down`; `totals` counts it in `failed_cost_usd`, `saved_usd` and `savings_pct` |
| `test_teacher_502_through_the_gateway_writes_one_row_with_the_student_cost` | The same through a live gateway: exactly one row (the router's), no URL or message in `error`, and `ratchet_cost_usd_total`, `ratchet_failed_cost_usd_total` and `ratchet_failures_total{stage="teacher"}` rise by it |
| `test_quota_429_writes_a_quota_row_and_counts_a_rejection` | A quota 429: a `quota` row with the client, task and id (`APIError 429 daily_quota_exceeded`, cost 0), one `quota` rejection, still no requests row |
| `test_teacher_429_is_a_teacher_failure_not_a_refusal` | A teacher 429 answered as a 429: one `teacher` row and no gateway `rate` row; no rejection counted; `ratchet_requests_total{status="429"}` once |
| `test_a_bug_on_the_teacher_path_is_a_gateway_failure_not_the_teachers` | A `RuntimeError` in `_store_example` after the student was sampled: a 500 and one `gateway` row (500, `RuntimeError`, the samples' cost), no `teacher` failure counted, the failed spend in the metrics and totals; with a throttle of 0 a row that carries spend is still written; in process, on `handle` and `teacher_only`, the router writes `gateway` rows with no status |
| `test_gateway_failure_row_exists_before_the_response_starts` | The app called as raw ASGI: when a wrong key's 401 starts, its `auth` row is already in the ledger under the response's `x-request-id` |
| `test_swallowed_failures_get_rows_and_breaker_refusals_are_sampled` | 30 requests against a failing student: 5 `student` rows for the errors (502), 3 for 25 breaker refusals (503), `ratchet_failures_total` counts all 30, every request answered; a timed-out audit: an `audit` row (504, `ReadTimeout`, cost 0) and the student's answer served |
| `test_stats_errors_last_hour_and_failed_cost` | `errors.last_hour` by stage leaves out a two-hour-old row; `totals.failed_cost_usd`; `log_failure` refuses an unknown stage or column |
| `test_gateway_failure_rows_are_throttled_per_stage` | `FailureThrottle` per stage and minute; five wrong keys write two rows but count five rejections and five failures |
| `test_json_log_lines_carry_no_content_and_no_keys` | A live gateway at INFO: no marker from a message or an answer, card number, API key, teacher key or endpoint host in any line; the `chat` lines have every expected key, the ids, the client name and trace id; the 502's line has `served_by` null; one `upstream_error` warning (role, class, status); a mirrored event cut to its exception class |
| `test_log_settings_formats_and_handlers` | `log_settings` defaults by mode, case-insensitive values, typos stop startup; `config` and `obs` agree on the values; `configure` never doubles a handler; the text format quotes values with spaces or quotes; JSON lines; levels filter |
| `test_event_listeners_see_every_event_and_cannot_fail_it` | A listener added twice runs once and gets the stored row; a failing listener does not stop the event |
| `test_every_version_transition_writes_an_event` | Cold start to canary to live, shadow to canary to live over the old live, a candidate replaced by a newer one, a rolled-back canary and a rejected candidate: all 14 moves (9 kinds of transition) go through `transition` or `promote_live` (`set_version` raises if the gate calls it) and each writes, inside the same call, an event naming the version, one of them `live_retired` |
| `test_metrics_parse_count_and_bound_their_labels` | `/metrics` parses, has `# TYPE` for every family and the 0.0.4 content type; a request counts under its task, an ad-hoc task as `other` (its name never a label), a 400 as `none`/`400` and an `invalid_request` rejection; an example counted by split; cost and baseline rise; `ratchet_info`; histogram buckets cumulative, ending at `+Inf` equal to `_count` |
| `test_cascade_metrics_for_upstreams_audits_and_examples` | Upstream `ok`, `timeout` and `circuit_open` outcomes, audits by result, escalations, stored examples and upstream latency observations counted exactly; `task_label` |
