| `test_per_task_off_critical_gate_disables_only_that_task` | FR-2.50, FR-14.1, FR-6.1: with `per_task=False` the eval gives `other_tasks`; a failing critical task is disabled and the version ships for its other task (rejected under `reject`); a passing critical task with 20% coverage of its own does not reject a version whose other task is fully served; a version whose other task serves nothing is rejected once the critical task is disabled |
| `test_rules_apply_to_task_precision_and_coverage` | FR-2.50, FR-14.1, FR-6.1: with every flagged answer sent to the teacher by a rule, the record (and the pooled numbers) show precision 0.9 and coverage 0.5 on what is served, and the task fails a 93% floor; wrong answers a rule always escalates do not keep the threshold from calibrating (0.9 instead of 1.01 without the rule) |
| `test_rescore_disables_failing_critical_task` | FR-2.50, FR-2.15, INV-4: a training night's re-check of the live version that finds 3 misses in 40 disables the task in the same eval write with a `task_disabled` event ("re-scored"); `rescore` without a gate leaves it; passing, missing and pinned records give no holds |
| `test_cascade_eval_skips_ineligible_rows` | FR-2.50, FR-5.2: an over-length golden row is not sampled and is counted in `excluded_ineligible`; the task keeps its record; without such rows the key is absent |
| `test_rule_must_reference_declared_field` | FR-2.50, FR-2.15: a rule naming `flaged` is one `SpecError` listing the task's fields; a field-less task's critical class says `its fields: none`; a `Task` built in code raises `ValueError`; a field with its own error is reported once |
| `test_routing_spec_errors_name_their_path` | FR-2.50, FR-2.15: 33 bad blocks (unknown keys, `audit_rate` with a hint, limits, content types, `store_ineligible`, list sizes, condition shapes, values outside a vocabulary, `action`, `min_confidence`, `max_miss_rate`, keys of the other list) each give their error with its path, from the spec parser and from `Task` |
| `test_routing_block_round_trips_and_extends_merges_it` | FR-2.50, FR-2.15: a block keeps its spelling and hash through `document`, its values normalised for the router; `extends` merges routing by key (null removes); a child dropping the field an inherited class names is refused; a `set` field cannot be a condition; the parser refuses NaN, ignores an empty block, and checks nothing against fields that are themselves in error |

### `tests/test_adjacent_endpoints.py`

The other OpenAI endpoints a drop-in base URL receives (FR-1.51, [ADR 0050](adr/0050-adjacent-openai-endpoints.md)): the `/v1/embeddings` pass-through, `/v1/completions` and the stateless subset of `/v1/responses` on the chat path, and the typed 404s. Mock-mode tests use the `gw_env` gateway; live-mode tests build a `Runtime` over `FakeOpenAIServer`s. Responses are checked with the OpenAI SDK's own models (`CreateEmbeddingResponse`, `Completion`, `Response`) and clients (`make_openai_client`).

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_mock_embeddings_validate_with_the_sdk` | FR-1.51, INV-12: deterministic unit vectors of 256 values in the SDK's shape, the same input giving the same vector; another `model` echoed in `X-Ratchet-Model-Alias`; `dimensions` keeps the full vector's direction; base64 (the SDK's default) decodes to the same floats; token arrays; the shared mock-mode gateway's cap on values per request; every call one row (task `embeddings`, served_by `upstream`, route `passthrough`, `stored` 0, cost = baseline = teacher column) and no example; the embeddings model in `/v1/models` and `GET /v1/models/{id}`, never `ratchet/embeddings` or an unregistered task |
| `test_embeddings_stay_out_of_the_headline_figures` | FR-1.51, FR-15.1: embeddings rows (route `passthrough`) leave `stats_by_day`, `totals` and `stats_by_client` unchanged (student share, latency and savings of the chat rows alone; a row without a route still counted), while the `embeddings` task row, the cost report and the key's quota count them; through the gateway, `/ratchet/stats` `days`, `totals` and `clients` are the same before and after three embeddings calls |
| `test_embeddings_validation_names_the_field` | FR-1.51: 19 bad bodies (missing, empty, mixed or non-integer inputs, negative token ids, booleans, more than 2,048 items, over `RATCHET_MAX_INPUT_CHARS` with a token as 4 characters, `encoding_format`, `dimensions`, `model`) each a 400 naming the field; bad JSON; a refused request logs nothing |
| `test_embeddings_model_policy_off_switch_and_quota` | FR-1.51, FR-1.50: under `strict` only `ratchet`, no model and the configured id are served (anything else a 404 before anything is logged); `RATCHET_EMBEDDINGS_BASE=off` is a 404 `unsupported_endpoint` and drops the model from `/v1/models`; embeddings and chat completions share the key's daily quota |
| `test_embeddings_settings_are_checked_at_startup` | FR-1.51: the teacher's base by default, `off` in any case, an http(s) URL; a bad base, an empty, padded or Ratchet-named model, or a model alias spelled like the embeddings model stops startup |
| `test_embeddings_model_is_priced_and_day1_checked` | FR-1.51, INV-9: every model in `EMBEDDING_PRICES` is in the day-1 check's list, has no output price, and is neither in `DEFAULT_PRICES` nor in the license registry |
| `test_live_embeddings_forward_the_configured_model` | FR-1.51, INV-1: whatever `model` a client sends, the upstream gets `RATCHET_EMBEDDINGS_MODEL` with the inputs and `dimensions` as sent and no `user` or `encoding_format`; usage prices the call; another base gets its own key and transport, an unpriced model the teacher's price, a keyless base no key at all (the teacher's own base, with a trailing slash, still gets `NEBIUS_API_KEY`); upstream 400, 401, 404, 429, 503, 504 and malformed bodies map to the client statuses and log nothing |
| `test_embeddings_task_name_is_reserved` | FR-1.51, FR-2.15, FR-1.50: a chat, completions or responses request naming `embeddings` (header, metadata, `ratchet/` or `ratchet:` model, Azure deployment) is a 400 `reserved_task` naming where it came from, before the quota; `GET /v1/models/ratchet/embeddings` is a 404; the admin API, a tasks file and a model alias cannot define it |
| `test_provider_parses_embeddings_strictly` | FR-1.51: vectors found by their `index`, base64 accepted, the upstream's model name and prompt tokens kept; 11 malformed bodies (wrong count, empty or uneven vectors, strings, booleans, values past float32, bad base64) are a 502 `bad_upstream_response` |
| `test_completions_answer_and_store_as_the_chat_request` | FR-1.51, INV-2, INV-12: a prompt through `/v1/completions` validates as the SDK's `Completion`, is answered as task `ticket-triage` from `model: ratchet/ticket-triage`, and stores exactly the example the chat request with that user message stores; the chat request then gets the same answer and usage and bumps that example |
| `test_completions_refuse_what_would_change_the_answer` | FR-1.51: `suffix`, `echo` and `best_of` that would change the answer are a 400 `unsupported_parameter`; token arrays, too many prompts, long prompts and bad `logprobs` are 400s naming the field; chat fields keep their names; an empty suffix, `echo: false` and `best_of` 1 or `n` are accepted; nothing refused is logged |
| `test_completions_batches_and_streams` | FR-1.51, FR-1.6: two prompts with `n: 2` give choices 0 to 3 in OpenAI's order, summed usage and one row per prompt; a batch the daily quota cannot finish is refused whole (`has 1 left`); the stream's `text_completion` chunks spell the non-streamed texts, end each choice with its finish reason, carry the usage chunk and the `ratchet` block, and end with `[DONE]` |
| `test_completions_batch_that_stops_after_its_first_prompt_is_not_retried` | FR-1.51, INV-3, INV-7: in live mode, a 2-prompt batch whose second teacher call fails (503 with `Retry-After`) is a 502 with `X-Should-Retry: false`, no `Retry-After` and a message naming the answered prompt, so the OpenAI SDK with `max_retries=2` sends it once and the first prompt's example has `seen` 1 (below FREQUENT), one row and one quota request; the first prompt's own failure keeps its `Retry-After` and chat message; a later prompt refused by a quota a concurrent request used up is a 429 the same way; a batch past `RATCHET_TEACHER_DEADLINE_S` stops before its next prompt (504 `batch_deadline_exceeded`); mock mode's plain 400 for a later prompt carries the header too |
| `test_completions_with_the_openai_sdk_and_legacy_logprobs` | FR-1.51, FR-1.14: the SDK's completions client in live mode, streamed and not; `logprobs: 2` returns tokens that spell the text, their logprobs, at most 2 alternatives and offsets from the prompt's length; the teacher gets the user message, the configured model, `logprobs`, `top_logprobs` and `max_tokens`, never `prompt` or `echo` |
| `test_legacy_logprobs_shape` | FR-1.51: the chat shape's token logprobs become `tokens`, `token_logprobs`, `top_logprobs` objects and `text_offset` |
| `test_responses_string_input_validates_with_the_sdk` | FR-1.51, INV-12: a string input with instructions validates as the SDK's `Response` with a non-empty `output_text`, echoes its settings (`store` false, no previous response), answers and stores exactly as the chat request [system, user]; the SDK's responses client reads it; a cut-off answer is `incomplete` with `max_output_tokens` |
| `test_responses_refuse_what_needs_state` | FR-1.51: `previous_response_id`, `conversation`, a stored `prompt`, `background` and `stream` true, built-in tools and tool choices are a 400 `unsupported_parameter`; item references, built-in tool items, files and images by id a 400 `unsupported_value`; 25 cases in all, each naming the Responses field (`max_output_tokens`, not `max_tokens`), among them a `reasoning.effort` the SDK does not know; fields with no effect are accepted; every effort the installed SDK knows is echoed and parses as its `Response`; the stored-response routes are a 404 `unsupported_endpoint` |
| `test_responses_input_items_become_chat_messages` | FR-1.51: message items with each role and part type, function calls and their outputs, reasoning items, instructions, `text.format`, function tools and choice, `max_output_tokens`, `temperature`, `reasoning.effort` and metadata become the chat request; chat-only fields are ignored and unknown ones kept; JSON-object and text formats; an image URL part |
| `test_responses_tool_loop_becomes_the_chat_conversation` | FR-1.51: a response's output sent back as input (its text, then its calls, with reasoning between them) is one assistant message with both calls, as the chat request of the same loop; calls after a tool result or a user message start their own assistant message |
| `test_response_output_carries_function_calls` | FR-1.51: a choice's tool calls become `function_call` items with their `call_id` (a generated one when empty, object arguments as JSON), after the message item when there is text; the object validates as the SDK's `Response`; usage details; `content_filter` is `incomplete` |
| `test_moderations_and_unknown_paths_get_typed_404s` | FR-1.51, FR-1.10: `/v1/moderations` is a 404 `unsupported_endpoint` with the docs link, which the SDK raises as `NotFoundError`; other `/v1/*` paths are `unknown_url`; a wrong method is a 405 in the envelope; with keys on every new route checks the key first |
| `test_adjacent_endpoints_reach_the_student` | FR-1.51: with a live version that knows the ticket, a completion and a response are served by the student, the same cascade as the chat request |

### `tests/test_sqlcheck.py`

Text-to-SQL tasks (FR-2.56, [ADR 0055](adr/0055-sql-tasks-compared-by-execution.md)): answers compared by what their queries return on a fixture, in a sandboxed in-memory SQLite database. One fixture script (`SHOP`: customers and orders, written like a `.dump`) serves most tests; tasks with the same fixture text share one sandbox, so tests that count executions or change limits use a script of their own. Running SQL needs Python 3.11 (`Connection.setlimit`), so the tests that do are marked `needs_sql` and skipped on 3.10; the path, postgres and refusal tests run everywhere.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_sql_equivalent_queries_agree` | FR-2.56: `COUNT(*)` and `count(id)`, a JOIN and an IN subquery (and EXISTS), aliases, SUM over integers and TOTAL, `0.1 + 0.2` and `0.3`, VALUES and UNION ALL agree; an executing sql task is verifiable, not free text, `exact` |
| `test_sql_wrong_filter_disagrees` | FR-2.56: another filter, `>` against `>=`, another column count, `1` against `'1'`, a duplicated row (a multiset) disagree |
| `test_sql_order_by_respected_otherwise_multiset` | FR-2.56: the outermost ORDER BY keeps rows in order; ORDER BY in a subquery, a CTE or a window does not; an ordered result never equals an unordered one of two rows or more; one row has one order; `ordered` `never` and `always` |
| `test_sql_empty_results_fall_back_to_text` | FR-2.56: two empty results agree only as the same normalised query (case, whitespace, comments, `;`); empty against not empty disagrees; `normalize`; `empty_result: agree` with the column count |
| `test_sql_multiple_statements_invalid` | FR-2.56: a second statement, prose after the query, unterminated strings, names and comments, EXPLAIN, PRAGMA, writes, a parenthesised start and a query over `MAX_QUERY_CHARS` are `not one query`; `;` and comments around one query, and `;` inside a string, are fine |
| `test_sql_answer_cleaning` | FR-2.56, FR-2.13: `<think>` and a ```` ```sql ```` fence are stripped (cleaning is idempotent); a fence inside the fence is kept and invalid; an empty or unfinished answer is `empty answer` |
| `test_sql_write_and_attach_denied_and_fixture_unchanged` | FR-2.56: writes that read as a query (`WITH ... INSERT`, `DELETE`, `UPDATE`) are `sql denied`; DELETE, INSERT, UPDATE, DROP, CREATE (TEMP too), ALTER, ATTACH, VACUUM INTO, BEGIN, SAVEPOINT, ANALYZE and PRAGMA run on the sandbox directly never return a result; no file is written; the row counts never change; with an authorizer that allows everything, `query_only` still refuses a write |
| `test_sql_pragma_denied` | FR-2.56: PRAGMA statements and the pragma table-valued functions are denied; reading `sqlite_master`, `sqlite_schema`, `json_each` and `json_tree` is allowed; `sqlite_stmt` (other requests' statements) and `dbstat` are `sql denied`, also through a fixture's view |
| `test_sql_runaway_recursive_cte_interrupted_quickly` | FR-2.56, NFR-1: an endless recursive CTE stops on `max_steps` in under 0.5 s, so does a nine-way cross join; a query within its steps but past `QUERY_SECONDS` stops too and is not cached (then runs with a generous time budget, so a busy machine cannot fail it) |
| `test_sql_random_function_denied` | FR-2.56: `random()` (in ORDER BY too), `randomblob`, `changes`, `total_changes`, `last_insert_rowid`, `load_extension`, `sqlite_version`, `zeroblob` are `sql denied`; `'now'`, `'NOW'`, `'localtime'` in a date function (also inside a nested call, a subquery or a CASE among its arguments), CURRENT_DATE/TIMESTAMP, a date function without a time value (`date()`, `julianday()`, `unixepoch()`, `strftime('%s')`), a quoted function name and a double-quoted `"now"` `reads the clock` in the text; a value built at run time (`'n' \|\| 'ow'`, a subquery, `subsec` as the time value, a `localtime` modifier built from parts, text after a NUL, a blob) is caught when the date function runs; dates from the data, `'subsec'` as a modifier, NULL and the string `'now'` outside a date function are fine; the wrapped date functions give SQLite's results; a clock-reading query is invalid under any spelling, never a cached result |
| `test_sql_rows_result_size_and_value_limits` | FR-2.56: more than `max_rows` rows is invalid, as many is fine; a result over `RESULT_BYTES` is `sql result too large` |
| `test_sql_value_limit` | FR-2.56: on Python 3.11+ a doubling string stops at `VALUE_BYTES` (`sql error`); skipped on 3.10 |
| `test_sql_quadratic_builtins_replaced_by_linear_ones` | FR-2.56, NFR-1: `trim`, `rtrim`, `instr` (text and blobs) and `replace` at `VALUE_BYTES` on inputs that are quadratic in SQLite all finish in under 2 s together (milliseconds each); a `replace` result over the cap and a large mixed-type `instr` are `sql error`; a LIKE pattern of `LIKE_PATTERN_BYTES` is refused and one just under it runs; `json_patch` and `unhex` are denied; a query that repeats the most expensive LIKE per row stops on `QUERY_SECONDS`, and so does one row whose WHERE calls it 400 times (LIKE checks the time on every call); LIKE with ESCAPE and GLOB give SQLite's results; on 3,000 random arguments per function (text, blobs, numbers, NULL, NUL characters, empty strings, long character sets) the Python versions give exactly SQLite's results and errors |
| `test_sql_memory_guard` | FR-2.56: a sorter filled with 2 KB rows is stopped by the resident-memory guard (4 MB here) and the outcome is not cached; with a generous time budget, so a busy machine cannot turn it into a timeout; Linux only (skipped where `/proc` cannot be read) |
| `test_sql_outcomes_cached_by_query_text` | FR-2.56: a result and an error run once for three checks; a statement over `CACHE_CHARS` runs every time; the cache holds at most `CACHE_SIZE` entries |
| `test_sql_threads_share_one_sandbox_safely` | FR-2.56: two tasks with the same fixture text share one sandbox; eight threads checking 60 answers each get the single-threaded keys and no error |
| `test_sql_spec_round_trip_and_spec_hash` | FR-2.56, FR-2.15: `parse_spec` and `document` round-trip the `sql` options (also through the ledger); the hash changes with an option or the fixture's text, not with its path (one sandbox); no other task's spec has the key; `extends` merges `sql` by key; RATCHET_FIXTURES_DIR is the default directory |
| `test_fixture_path_outside_dir_rejected` | FR-2.56, FR-2.15: `..`, an absolute path, a symlink out and `sub/../..` are spec errors at `sql.fixture`; `SqlSpec.load` refuses them too |
| `test_sql_spec_errors_have_paths` | FR-2.56, FR-2.15: a document of 21 tasks with 27 mistakes (no `sql` for format sql, `sql` on a JSON task, fields on a sql task, every option out of range or unknown, postgres with a fixture, no fixture, not an object, a missing, broken, attaching, pragma-setting, random-using, tableless, Latin-1, oversized or directory fixture, a reserved format, a fixture whose load reads the clock, directly or through a `DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, one whose DEFAULT calls `randomblob()`, one whose view reads the clock) gives exactly those errors in order; a FIFO is not read; `Task` and `SqlSpec` built in code refuse the same |
| `test_fixture_load_limits` | FR-2.56: a `.dump` (PRAGMA foreign_keys, BEGIN, COMMIT), an unfinished transaction and clock-reading defaults that no row takes load; a value over `VALUE_BYTES`, a fixture over `MAX_FIXTURE_DB_BYTES` once loaded and one past `LOAD_SECONDS` are refused |
| `test_sql_execution_needs_python_311` | FR-2.56: `CAN_LIMIT` is true from Python 3.11; without it a sandbox is refused and a sqlite spec is a `sql.fixture` error that says why and names `postgres`; a postgres task still works (runs on 3.10 too); uses an empty sandbox map, so no sandbox another test left alive answers for it |
| `test_sql_postgres_dialect_compares_text_only` | FR-2.56: normalised text only, nothing runs (so no clock check); not verifiable, free text; parses and round-trips |
| `test_sql_task_in_the_cascade` | FR-2.56, FR-2.13, INV-16: three texts of one query let the student answer, unfenced; disagreeing or failing samples escalate and the teacher's answer is stored without its fence; a teacher answer that is denied, fails or reads the clock (`date('now')`, `julianday()`, `date("now", ...)`) is served, not stored, and `skip_example` names only the reason |
| `test_sql_golden_eval_and_dataset` | FR-2.56, INV-6: `cascade_eval` skips references that fail on the fixture (`golden_invalid_refs`) and scores equivalent student queries as agreeing; `dataset.rows` leaves stored answers that fail out (`skipped_invalid`) |
| `test_sql_task_through_the_admin_api_and_tasks_file` | FR-2.56, FR-2.15: PUT creates a sql task (verifiable, its `sql` shown); the same body is no change until the fixture's text changes, then a new spec_hash and a `task_changed` event; a path outside the directory is a 422; validate; a chat request; a tasks file with a sql task starts, and one whose fixture is gone stops startup with the path |


### `tests/test_redaction.py`

Redaction for extraction tasks, the detector plugin and Indian identifiers (FR-13.7, FR-13.8, FR-4.7, NFR-4, INV-12, [ADR 0082](adr/0082-placeholder-styles-detector-plugin-and-indian-identifiers.md)). Detector plugins for the tests are in `tests/redact_plugins.py` (a working one, ones that fail at store time or return bad spans, ones that fail the startup probe); nothing imports Presidio.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_two_emails_distinct_placeholders` | FR-13.7: `label` loses which email is which; `numbered` and `surrogate` keep them apart, repeats and case included, every placeholder counted; numbers per label and per example; a phone with and without its country code, and a card with spaces or dashes, is one value; an unknown style refused |
| `test_numbered_placeholders_consistent_between_prompt_and_answer` | FR-13.7, INV-12: the teacher sees the original; the stored prompt has `[EMAIL_1]`, `[EMAIL_2]`, `[PHONE_1]` and the stored answer names `[EMAIL_2]`; the comparator tells the right placeholder from the wrong one |
| `test_one_pseudonymizer_covers_messages_answer_and_conditions` | FR-13.7: content parts, `name`, `refusal`, tool-call arguments, the answer and the conditions share one numbering; the caller's messages are untouched |
| `test_surrogate_values_stable_under_second_pass` | FR-13.7: `redact(redact(x)) == redact(x)` in every style for a text with one of each identifier; surrogates are `example.com`, 555-01xx in E.164, RFC 5737, valid `ZZ` IBANs and card numbers that fail Luhn and hold no Aadhaar; no original left |
| `test_surrogates_are_reserved_and_distinct_until_they_run_out` | FR-13.7: 300 emails, 79,800 phones, 999 cards, IBANs and 762 IPs distinct and reserved, then the numbered fallback |
| `test_label_style_matches_v04` | FR-13.7, FR-4.7, NFR-4: the nine v0.4 rules' SHA-256; on 4,500 fuzzed texts exactly v0.4's spans and v0.4's sequential replacement with today's rules, and on those without GSTIN or UPI ids exactly v0.4's output with v0.4's rules; on 8,000 long reference numbers (5 to 8 groups of four digits, spaced, dashed or unspaced) exactly v0.4's output; numbered placeholders find the same spans |
| `test_a_refused_match_no_longer_hides_a_card` | FR-4.7, NFR-4, INV-12: a card with its CVV or expiry, two cards in a row, a card before a GSTIN, and a 16-character IBAN before a year or a short word are found where v0.4 left them; a card with digits or a word before it on its run, more than 4 digits after it, a tail that joins an IP address or a phone number, and a non-Luhn run stay as v0.4 left them; on 3,000 fuzzed texts, wherever the output differs from v0.4's it is that rule and nothing else; numbered and surrogate alike |
| `test_redaction_is_idempotent_on_realistic_text` | FR-13.7, FR-4.7: 3,000 texts of identifiers and words between ordinary separators, never two numbers one space apart (the known exception its docstring names), redacted twice, are unchanged in every style |
| `test_default_settings_store_v04_examples` | FR-13.7, FR-4.7: with the default settings the stored example is v0.4's byte for byte |
| `test_gstin_and_upi_ids_are_redacted` | FR-13.8, FR-4.7, NFR-4: GSTINs (also entity `A` and a company PAN) and UPI ids (`name@okicici`, `9876543210@ybl`, a sentence's full stop) replaced; package tags, `a+b@ybl`, one-letter handles, a space after `@` and a 14-character near-GSTIN kept |
| `test_redact_plugin_detector_applied` | FR-13.7, NFR-4: a fake NER detector tags names, numbered with the emails; label and surrogate styles; an empty text never reaches the plugin; a dotted path, a lower-case label and offsets of an integer type that is not `int` (numpy's) |
| `test_detector_spans_merge_with_patterns_earlier_then_longer` | FR-13.7: overlapping spans: the earlier wins, then the longer, then the pattern's, covering all it overlaps; touching spans stay apart |
| `test_missing_plugin_fails_startup` (9 cases) | FR-13.7, NFR-4: a missing module or attribute, a non-callable, a malformed spec and a detector failing the empty-text probe stop `load_detector`, `Ratchet` and the gateway's `Runtime` |
| `test_bad_style_fails_startup_and_style_is_read_loosely` | FR-13.7: an unknown style or a non-string plugin stops `Ratchet`; case and spaces are ignored and empty means `label`; both settings read from the environment |
| `test_a_failing_detector_stores_nothing_and_never_leaks_the_text` (7 cases) | FR-13.7, INV-12, INV-16: a detector that raises, returns an out-of-range, empty or malformed span, bool or float offsets, or a bad label: the answer is served, nothing stored, one `skip_example` `redaction failed`, no table holds the text, and the error carries neither the text nor the plugin's message |
| `test_original_values_never_stored` (3 cases) | FR-13.7, NFR-4, INV-12: in each style, with the plugin and a stop string, no original value reaches any table; numbered placeholders in the answer match the prompt's |
| `test_task_spec_privacy_redact_style_overrides_the_default` | FR-13.7, FR-2.15: `privacy.redact_style` parsed, documented and round-tripped, outside `spec_hash`; bad values and keys listed with their paths; `extends` merges it (null removes it); the cascade stores that task numbered and another task labelled |
| `test_admin_api_sets_a_tasks_style_without_recalibrating` | FR-13.7, FR-2.15: a `PUT` that only sets `privacy.redact_style` changes the task, keeps its `spec_hash` (not recalibrating), shows in `/ratchet/stats` and writes a `task_changed` event saying the hash was kept; a bad style is a 422 with its path |
| `test_changing_the_style_stores_a_new_example_once` | FR-13.7: the same request under `label` twice and `numbered` twice is two examples seen twice each |
| `test_an_erasure_holds_under_every_style_and_plugin` | FR-13.3, FR-13.7, INV-12: an example erased under each of five style and plugin settings is refused under all five; both its hash and its key under the default redaction (`canon_hash`) are tombstoned; default rows record no `canon_hash` |
| `test_long_reference_numbers_store_as_in_v04` | FR-4.7, NFR-4, INV-12, INV-3: 40 tracking numbers in a prompt and unspaced in the answer are stored exactly as v0.4 stored them, with v0.4's example hash, so the target never holds digits its prompt lacks |
| `test_stored_conditions_are_numbered_in_key_order` | FR-13.7, INV-12, INV-3: conditions that differ only in key order get the same numbering and the same example hash; each copy keeps the client's key order |
| `test_the_example_hash_never_depends_on_the_answer` | FR-13.7, INV-3: three different answers to one request with a stop string are one example seen three times; the conditions are numbered before the answer |
| `test_concurrent_examples_never_share_placeholders` | FR-13.7, INV-12: 16 threads each store an example that starts at `[EMAIL_1]` and names its own `[EMAIL_2]` |
| `test_redact_preview_shows_the_style_and_takes_style` | FR-13.7: `POST /ratchet/redact` in the gateway's style, with the plugin for an API or admin key and without it (`plugin_skipped`) for no key or a wrong one, `?style=` in any case, 400 for an unknown style; `/ratchet/stats` `privacy` style keys, a task's override included |
| `test_redact_preview_reports_a_failing_plugin_without_the_text` | FR-13.7: a failing plugin is a 500 naming its exception class only, for a caller with a key; a keyless preview never reaches it; without a plugin, names are left |
| `test_gateway_stores_numbered_examples_end_to_end` | FR-13.7, INV-12: over HTTP under `RATCHET_REDACT_STYLE=numbered` the stored prompt and answer share placeholders |
| `test_every_pattern_scans_adversarial_input_in_linear_time` | NFR-5, FR-4.7: every pattern in `PATTERNS`, and `redact` as a whole, finish 27 hostile inputs of 120,000 characters (runs of address, digit and separator characters) within 3 s (9 s for `redact`); the unbounded EMAIL pattern took 20 to 30 s |
| `test_email_pattern_still_finds_addresses_and_cuts_a_long_local_part` | FR-4.7: plus-tags, dotted domains and two addresses in a row are found; a 64-character local part is an address, a 100-character one is not |

### `tests/test_kpis.py`

Success metrics (FR-7.9, SM-1 to SM-5, NFR-1, [ADR 0083](adr/0083-success-metrics-from-the-ledger.md)). Each metric is checked on synthetic day rows built in the test, and the ledger reads against brute force over request rows the test logged; the reference run's metrics are in `test_system.py`.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_kpis_unit` | SM-1, SM-2, SM-3, SM-5, FR-7.9: the window is the last W days with traffic (a quiet day skipped, fewer days, bad sizes refused); SM-1 with and without failed spend, a share at the target counting whatever the float rounding, no baseline as `None`; SM-3; SM-2's pooled point estimate with `stats.wilson`'s bounds, `None` below the minimum audits or without audits, exactly 95% met, bad counts refused; SM-5 episodes (3 nights met, 4 not, open within the target `None`, open past it not met, the longest of two, none at all 0, too few days `None`) |
| `test_drift_episode_boundaries_and_what_counts_as_a_day` | SM-5: exactly 5 points above the baseline starts an episode and exactly 2 points above ends it; a slow climb never starts one; days with fewer than 50 judged requests are skipped and nights count calendar days; the rate ignores escalations the student never judged (an outage) |
| `test_bad_deploys_count_versions_found_bad_at_full_traffic` | SM-4: a canary rolled back before 100% does not count; an alert on a live version does (also in the anytime-valid wording, without a logged promotion, repeated); a manual rollback after 100% by its event or by the version's state; `None` before any version reached 100%; events that name no version ignored |
| `test_report_reads_the_ledger_and_survives_folding` | SM-1, SM-2, SM-3, NFR-1, FR-7.9, FR-13.4: `report` over six days of logged rows equals brute force (savings, share, pooled audits, nearest-rank median latency); `kpi_days`' judged and judged-escalated counts; folding days 1 to 2.5 and then everything into `daily_rollup` keeps every day's sums and the audits, while latency reads only the rows still kept; a `failures` table's spend counts against SM-1 inside the window only |
| `test_latency_quantile_is_nearest_rank` | NFR-1, FR-7.9: every quantile from 0 to 1 against the nearest-rank rule, per server and per day range, no rows as `None`, bad arguments refused; once `requests.wall_ms` exists it is read where a row in the range recorded it, `latency_ms` otherwise |
| `test_report_on_an_empty_or_odd_ledger_stays_valid_json` | FR-7.9, SM-4: an empty ledger (every metric unknown, SM-4 0); a version live now and a promotion event count as reaching 100%, an alert then makes it a bad deploy; an infinite cost gives `None`, never invalid JSON; bad arguments refused |
| `test_report_while_requests_are_logged` | FR-7.9: two threads build reports while four log 600 requests; no error, every report valid JSON with a request count between the bounds, and the final count exact |
| `test_kpis_endpoint_shape` | FR-7.9, SM-1 to SM-5, NFR-1: `GET /ratchet/kpis` on a fresh mock gateway (all unknown) and after three simulated days: `simulated`, the window, every metric's keys and types, the targets equal to `SM_TARGETS`, SM-2's minimum the gate's, a version at 100%; the same report as `/ratchet/stats`' `kpis` block; `?days=1` and a window larger than the ledger; 400 for 0, -2 and 3,651, 422 for a non-number |
| `test_bad_kpi_window_stops_startup` (5 cases) | FR-7.9: `RATCHET_KPI_WINDOW_DAYS` of 0, -3, 3651, `two` and `1.5` stop the gateway's `Runtime` naming the variable; 7 and the default 3 are read |

### `tests/test_drift.py`

The label-free drift alarm (FR-16.6, FR-6.40, FR-6.41, NFR-55, NFR-56, [ADR 0032](adr/0032-label-free-drift-alarm.md)). Simulation numbers are from mock models.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_fenwick_ranks_match_a_sorted_list_with_ties` | `Fenwick.prefix` equals `bisect` on a sorted list over 3,000 inserts with many values per bin (the quantised ties) and at both ends of the range; a bin outside the tree and an empty tree raise |
| `test_bins_and_usable_confidences` | Confidence 0 maps to bin 0 and 1 to bin 10,000, out-of-range values clamp; NaN, infinities, booleans and strings are not usable and `observe` refuses them without counting; a threshold of 1 or less is refused |
| `test_p_values_are_uniform_under_the_null` (2 cases) | 5,000 i.i.d. confidences give smoothed conformal p-values flat over [0, 1] (each decile within 4 binomial standard deviations of 500), also when the scores take only 9 values |
| `test_rarely_alarms_under_the_null` | Ville's bound, quickly: 100 seeded i.i.d. streams of 300 points at threshold 10 alarm at most 10% of the time |
| `test_rarely_alarms_under_the_null_full_size` | Slow: 200 seeded i.i.d. streams of 2,000 points at threshold 100: at most 3% ever alarm (the bound is 1%) |
| `test_alarms_soon_after_confidence_drops` | A stream whose confidence drops at n = 1,000 (30% of requests new to the student) alarms within 500 points and never before the drop; the same stream without the drop never alarms |
| `test_rising_confidence_makes_the_capital_fall` | One-sided: confidence that rises after n = 1,000 never alarms and the capital ends below where it was |
| `test_a_late_change_is_caught_as_fast_as_an_early_one` | The switching rate falls as 1/n after 100 points: before a drop at 20,000 the capital stays above 10^-6, and the drop alarms within 1,000 points |
| `test_alarm_latches_and_the_trajectory_stays_small` | Only the first crossing reports, with its request id and day; the trajectory keeps under 200 evenly spaced points by stride doubling; the summary's fields |
| `test_ten_thousand_observations_take_under_150_ms` | NFR-55: 10,000 `observe` calls in under 150 ms at the speed of the laptop the bound was set on: best of five rounds, each scaled by how much slower a fixed loop of the betting arithmetic ran just before (coverage's tracer and a slow runner made it 156 to 163 ms on CI) |
| `test_boosted_rate_is_capped_and_never_lowers_a_rate` | `boosted_rate`: the rate times the boost, at most 0.5, never below the rate itself |
| `test_confidence_stream_reads_only_student_confidences_in_id_order` | `Ledger.confidence_stream` returns rows with a confidence and a student version only (no cold start, no failed student call, no pinned task), in id order, after an id, up to a limit, and narrowed by version, task and an id bound; `version_states` |
| `test_events_once_and_the_last_event` | `Ledger.event_keyed` writes an event under a key once, also from a second connection to the same file, and records its id in `meta` with extra entries; `day` dates it; `last_event` is `None` without one; `request_ts` |
| `test_replaying_the_ledger_reproduces_every_capital_exactly` | A monitor fed tick by tick, one that replays the whole ledger at once and one that reads it in batches of 50 hold the same numbers to the last bit, alarms included; `rebuild` reproduces them; another seed gives other numbers |
| `test_compaction_keeps_serving_versions_and_replays_a_restored_one` | Counts are kept for canary, live and the most recently retired version; an older retired or rolled-back one keeps only its final numbers and refuses to observe; restored to live by hand, its stream is replayed first and matches a fresh replay |
| `test_compaction_keeps_the_most_recently_retired_version_not_the_newest` | A canary retired early has a higher id than the live version retired later; the later one (the one an operator would restore) keeps its counts |
| `test_rows_without_a_finite_confidence_are_skipped` | Infinite confidences are counted as skipped and never observed |
| `test_stream_memory_follows_what_it_has_seen` | NFR-56: a stream that saw one confidence holds under 8 KB; the sorted array of bins and the Fenwick tree (forced from the first observation) give the same capital, alarm and trajectory to the last bit; `Fenwick.build` equals one insert per value; `compact` drops the counts and the generator |
| `test_client_task_names_cannot_grow_the_monitor` | NFR-56, threat model T12: 1,000 invented task names hold 17 streams (16 by first appearance and `*`) in under 250 KB; 5,000 more add no stream and under 150 KB; an early name keeps its stream; another version gets its own 16; a replay agrees; `max_streams=0` pools every task |
| `test_a_compacted_shared_stream_replays_its_own_rows` | A compacted version restored by hand replays its `*` stream from its rows whose task has no stream of its own; a new name still goes to it; the result matches a fresh replay |
| `test_tick_writes_one_drift_event_per_alarm_across_restarts` | An alarm writes one `drift` event naming the version, the task, the evidence and both false-alarm bounds, dated on the alarm's day with its request and day in `meta`; later ticks and a second gate replaying the same file write no other; `drift_status` and `open_alarm` |
| `test_a_shift_under_invented_task_names_alarms_on_the_shared_stream` | NFR-56: a drop in confidence spread over 1,500 invented names alarms on the version's `*` stream, and the event calls it "other tasks" |
| `test_a_drift_event_the_ledger_refused_is_written_by_the_next_check` | A busy database refuses the event: the error propagates, the alarm stays latched, and the next check writes the event once, dated on the alarm's day |
| `test_policy_turns_the_monitor_off_and_refuses_actions_without_it` | `RATCHET_DRIFT_ALARM=0` turns the monitor off (no events, no streams, the off status); thresholds of 1 or less, a boost below 1, an action with the monitor off and a switch other than 0 or 1 are refused; the three variables are read by `from_env` |
| `test_certificate_is_suspended_while_a_drift_alarm_is_open` | FR-16.3: `GET /ratchet/versions/{id}/certificate` is `certified` with a quiet stream (its `drift` lists the streams without trajectories), `suspended` while an alarm is open (the certificate still shown), and `drift` is `null` for a version that never served |
| `test_drift_endpoint_and_stats_block` | `GET /ratchet/drift` with every key, a stream's fields, `?version=` filtering and a 422 for a bad value; the `drift` block of `/ratchet/stats`; both with the monitor off |
| `test_task_headers_sent_to_the_gateway_hold_a_bounded_number_of_streams` | NFR-56, threat model T12: 40 unauthenticated requests to the mock gateway, each with its own `X-Ratchet-Task`, are distilled, yet the monitor holds 17 streams for the version (the 24 later names in `*`); `/ratchet/stats` reports 17 and a family bound of 0.17 |
| `test_a_restarted_gateway_replays_the_ledger_at_startup` | A live `Runtime` on an existing ledger file replays it when it starts, writes no second event for an alarm already recorded, and shares its audit boosts with the router |
| `test_sqlite_stores_no_nan` | A NaN confidence is stored as NULL, which `confidence_stream` leaves out |
| `test_an_up_to_date_monitor_reads_one_query_per_update` | NFR-55: after its replay an update is one range query; the versions' states are read again only every 1,000 rows |
| `test_concurrent_checks_write_each_alarm_once` | Six threads checking drift at once observe each row once, write the alarm once and leave the capital of a plain replay and the boost in place |
| `test_audit_boost_raises_only_the_alarmed_versions_rate_with_one_draw_per_answer` | FR-6.40: a boosted canary is audited at 0.3 (0.1 × 3) and at 0.5 with a boost of 10 (the cap), the live version at 0.1, with the same 800 draws and the same requests reaching each version as without a boost; `label_p` records each rate |
| `test_gate_boosts_an_alarmed_serving_version_until_a_newer_one_goes_live` | FR-6.40: off by default; with a boost of 3 the alarmed live version is boosted in the dict the router holds, and cleared (in place) once a newer version is live |
| `test_early_night_halves_the_new_examples_needed_only_after_an_alarm` | FR-6.41, INV-5: 30 new examples do not train without an alarm, nor with the action off after one; with it on they train (the `trained` event says it was an early night); once a night has trained, 30 more need the usual 50 again |
| `test_early_night_never_trains_while_a_release_is_in_flight` | FR-6.41, INV-5: an alarm on a canary does not let Night School train |
| `test_an_alarm_found_by_a_replay_keeps_its_day_and_counts_only_after_the_last_training` | FR-6.41: a replay finds an alarm from day 3, before the last training on day 9; its event is dated day 3 and brings no early night although its id is newer; a change after that training, found only by Night School's own check, halves the examples needed |
| `test_sim_alarms_on_the_launch_day` | FR-9.4: the ticket launch on day 5 alarms on day 5 or 6, never before (the seed-7 reference run at 2,000 a day, whose corrupted v3 raises no alarm, and seed 11 at 1,000 a day) |
| `test_sim_never_alarms_without_drift` (2 cases) | Seeds 7 and 11, 6 days × 1,000 requests without drift: no `drift` event |
| `test_monitor_draws_nothing_from_the_router_rng` | NFR-55: the same seeded run with the monitor on and off gives the same days, versions, totals and events, apart from the `drift` event |
| `test_sim_alarm_sweep` (5 cases) | Slow: seeds 1, 2, 3, 7 and 11 at 2,000 a day alarm on day 5 or 6 after the launch and never in 6 days without one |
| `test_sim_flags_reach_the_policy_and_the_router` | `--drift-alarm`, `--drift-boost` and `--drift-early-night` set the gate's policy and the router shares the gate's boosts; settings that would do nothing are refused; the table prints the drift line and the event |
| `test_false_alarm_bounds_keep_three_significant_digits` | `drift.pct` keeps 3 significant digits, and the simulator's drift line at threshold 1000 says 0.1%, not 0% |

### `tests/test_teacher_watch.py`

The teacher watch (FR-16.7, FR-5.40, FR-5.41, FR-6.42, FR-6.43, NFR-57, [ADR 0033](adr/0033-teacher-watch.md)). Simulation numbers are from mock models.

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_a_disagreeing_label_is_checked_contested_and_left_out_of_both_splits` (2 cases) | A duplicate with another label writes one `teacher_checks` row (`agree` 0, source `duplicate`, epoch 1), marks the row contested and keeps the stored label; `dataset.rows` leaves it out of the train and the golden split (`excluded_contested`), and keeps it with `exclude_contested=False` |
| `test_the_same_label_is_an_agreeing_check` | Other reply text with the same fields is an agreeing check; the row stays in training |
| `test_free_text_pinned_and_unregistered_tasks_write_no_check` (3 cases) | `default` (no fields), `research-plan` (pinned, INV-10) and an unregistered name are never checked or contested |
| `test_labels_that_needed_redaction_are_never_checked` | A stored or a new label with placeholders writes no check (INV-12); a clean re-test of a clean stored label still does |
| `test_first_re_test_rule_one_check_per_request_per_epoch` | Ten re-labels in one epoch give one check; an ack and a model change each allow one more |
| `test_a_contested_or_adjudicated_label_is_never_compared_again` | After an ack, a contested label and an adjudicated one (UNCHECKABLE) are compared by no duplicate and offered to no probe; only adjudication lifts a quarantine |
| `test_labels_from_another_teacher_or_sampled_labels_are_not_compared` | Another configured teacher's label and a label sampled above temperature 0, stored or as a re-test, are not compared; sampled rows are UNCHECKABLE and never probe candidates |
| `test_a_stored_label_the_current_spec_refuses_is_not_compared` | A stored label a tightened spec refuses is no evidence about the teacher |
| `test_router_stores_sampled_teacher_answers_as_uncheckable` | `comparable_label`: temperature 0 (or unset) without a reasoning effort only; the router stores a sampled answer as UNCHECKABLE and checks two greedy ones |
| `test_the_freeze_rule_rarely_alarms_when_the_teacher_is_within_its_noise` (2 cases) | Ville's bound by seeded Monte Carlo: 300 runs of 400 checks over 10 days at 5% and 3% disagreement alarm in at most 1% plus three standard errors |
| `test_the_freeze_rule_catches_a_teacher_four_times_too_noisy_within_300_checks` | Power: at 20% every run alarms within 300 checks, the median under 100 |
| `test_reusing_a_stored_label_breaks_the_guarantee_the_first_re_test_keeps` | Requests whose two draws disagree exactly 5% of the time: comparing the stored label with ten re-tests alarms in over 15% of runs (about 30%); the first re-test alone stays within the bound |
| `test_day_restarts_catch_a_teacher_that_changes_mid_epoch` | After 400 agreeing checks over 14 days, 15% disagreement alarms within 150 checks with the day mixture; a single e-process does not within 200 |
| `test_evidence_is_a_mixture_over_day_starts_whose_weights_sum_to_one` | The weights 1/(j(j+1)) sum to 1; `evidence` equals the hand-computed mixture (days without checks share the next day's counts; order and repeated days do not matter); a younger epoch weighs a later stretch more |
| `test_the_ceiling_bounds_any_students_agreement_at_full_coverage` | `self_agreement` and `ceiling` on known distributions; max p ≤ sqrt(sum p²) on 2,000 random distributions and E[max p] ≤ sqrt(E[a]); `summary` |
| `test_the_golden_gate_warns_when_the_floor_is_above_the_ceiling` | FR-6.43: 30 of 40 agreeing checks write an advisory `warning` naming the ceiling, the floor and the counts, and the candidate is still admitted; 29 checks, or the watch off, write none |
| `test_the_gate_freezes_night_school_once_and_an_ack_resumes_it` | FR-6.42, NFR-57: 12 of 40 disagreeing checks freeze it with one `teacher_drift` event; a second process finds it frozen and writes none; a quiet tick reads two SELECTs; an ack from the other process starts epoch 2, unfreezes and resets the gate's counts |
| `test_the_watch_switch_turns_off_every_action` | `teacher_watch=0`: no freeze, the checks still reported |
| `test_policy_checks_the_teacher_watch_fields` | The alphas and the switch are refused out of range, naming the variable |
| `test_settings_check_the_nightly_limits` | `RATCHET_TEACHER_PROBES` and `RATCHET_ADJUDICATE_MAX` from 0 to 10,000, whole numbers; `Ratchet` stops on a bad one |
| `test_a_changed_teacher_model_id_writes_one_event_and_starts_an_epoch` | One `teacher_model_changed` event and epoch 2 when the reported id changes (empty ids ignored); a restarted router reads the id from `meta`; a 500-character id is kept to 200 |
| `test_a_teacher_alternating_between_two_model_ids_starts_one_epoch` | Twenty responses alternating between two ids write one event; a third id one more; an ack keeps only the last id, so the other is new again |
| `test_a_ledger_that_refuses_the_model_id_never_fails_the_teachers_answer` | A locked ledger in `teacher_model_seen` still serves the answer; the next response records the id |
| `test_the_fallback_teachers_model_id_is_not_watched` | A fallback teacher's answer records no id and no event |
| `test_concurrent_requests_see_a_model_change_once` | 16 threads that all see a new id write one event and one epoch |
| `test_probes_re_ask_unchecked_stored_labels_in_hash_order_as_first_re_tests` | FR-5.40: only checkable rows are candidates, in hash order; two probes ask under the stored conditions, record `probe` checks, contest a disagreement and write the `teacher_watch` event; a later duplicate of a probed row is no check |
| `test_a_probe_answer_that_could_not_be_stored_is_no_check` | `usable_label` (reasoning stripped; cut off, invalid or needing a placeholder refused, INV-16, INV-12); such a probe is counted unusable, writes no check and a quiet night no event |
| `test_adjudication_two_of_three_resolves_and_one_one_one_stays_contested` | FR-5.41: the stored label kept, a 2-of-3 majority replacing it, and two without a majority left UNRESOLVED; resolved labels are UNCHECKABLE; provenance, license and redactions unchanged (INV-1, INV-2); `majority` |
| `test_labels_never_adjudicated_go_before_unresolved_ones` | A fresh contested label is adjudicated before three unresolved ones |
| `test_a_failing_teacher_stops_the_nights_watch_work_but_not_the_night` | A connection error stops probes and adjudication; the event names the class, never the message |
| `test_a_frozen_watch_skips_the_night_before_any_teacher_call` | INV-5: a frozen night writes `skip` and makes no teacher call or training; after an ack it trains on every row that is not contested |
| `test_probes_run_while_a_release_is_in_flight_but_nothing_trains` | Probes are evidence: they run during a shadow, and the night still skips training |
| `test_the_bom_records_the_teachers_self_agreement_and_the_labels_left_out` | The BOM's `teacher` section; with the watch off no section and the contested rows trained on |
| `test_unreadable_meta_values_fall_back_and_the_module_check_freezes` | An epoch, a day or a list of ids in `meta` that does not parse reads as its default; `teacher_watch.check` freezes once without a gate |
| `test_concurrent_duplicates_give_one_check_per_epoch` | Eight threads adding 200 duplicates write one check; a probe and a duplicate from two connections to one file check an example once; a bad source is refused |
| `test_an_old_file_gains_the_columns_and_its_rows_are_checked` | A v0.4 examples table gains `contested` and `checked_epoch` (0) and its rows are checked; the state defaults to epoch 1, not frozen, day 1 |
| `test_redaction_placeholders_never_create_false_disagreements` | Two tickets that differ only in an email address share one stored row; their different labels write no check |
| `test_mock_teacher_noise_is_reproducible_and_absent_at_zero` | Noise 0 draws nothing; 0.15 changes about 15% of first labels, the same in two instances; 1.0 always changes the category to another valid one and never the priority |
| `test_teacher_endpoints_report_and_ack_in_mock_mode` | `GET /ratchet/teacher`'s keys and sources, the demo fault (400 or 422 outside 0 to 1), a noisy cold-start day freezing it, the `teacher_watch` block of `/ratchet/stats`, the ack (`mock-admin`) and a fresh epoch |
| `test_teacher_ack_needs_an_admin_key_in_live_mode` | INV-8, INV-13: in live mode the ack is 403 without admin keys, 401 without a key, 403 with an API key, and works with an admin key (its name in the event, never the key); the demo fault is mock-only; `GET /ratchet/teacher` is public |
| `test_sim_a_teacher_noisy_from_day_3_freezes_night_school` | FR-9.1: seed 2, 6 days of 1,000 tickets, noise 0.15 from day 3: one `teacher_drift` on day 3 or later, no training after it, the strongest stretch from day 3 or later; the table's teacher line |
| `test_sim_a_stable_teacher_moves_no_number` | NFR-57: the same run at noise 0 with the watch on and off has the same days, versions and events, no teacher event, and more than 50 checks, none disagreeing |
| `test_the_reference_run_has_checks_and_no_teacher_event` | The pinned reference run checks the teacher (over 100 checks, no disagreement, e-value below 1) and writes no teacher event |
| `test_sim_never_freezes_a_stable_teacher_full_size` | Slow: five seeds of 10 days at 2,000 a day never freeze a stable teacher |

## Live-path tests

Live mode, the training job, the day-1 check, the Scout CLI and the deploy scripts would otherwise run for the first time on paid Nebius resources. `tests/test_live.py` runs each of them offline first, through seams that are default arguments, so production code passes nothing and talks to the real services:

| Seam | Where | Test double |
|---|---|---|
| `transport=` | `OpenAICompatProvider(base_url, api_key, timeout, retry, transport, sleep, clock=, rng=)` | `FakeOpenAIServer(...).transport` (an `httpx.MockTransport`); `sleep=`, `clock=` and `rng=` replace the waits between retries |
| `transports=` | `Runtime(settings, transports={"teacher": ..., "student": ...})`, live mode only (other keys raise) | the same; later items drive live mode offline through it |
| `client=` / `http=` | `load_adapter`, `NebiusTrainer`, `reload_adapters`, `live.main`, `http_caller`, the Scout CLI's `main(argv, client)`, `day1_check.main(client)` | an `httpx.Client` on a fake server, or on `AppTransport(gateway.app)` |
| `run=` | `NebiusTrainer`, `live.main` (in place of `subprocess.run`) | `RecordingRun` |
| `PATH` | `deploy/train_job.sh`, `deploy/student_endpoint.sh` (the `nebius` they call) | `FakeNebius`: logs each call's argv, prints `tests/fixtures/nebius_endpoint_list.json` for `ai endpoint list`; a stub `ratchet.cost` in the working directory logs the uptime records |
| `provider_factory=` | `live.main` (builds the student and judge clients) | a factory returning providers on fake servers |
| `argv=` | `sim.simulate.main(argv)`, the Scout CLI | a list |

The student's sampling parameters are one read-only constant, `confidence.STUDENT_SAMPLING` (`temperature` 0.7, `logprobs` on), used by `Ratchet._sample`, `LiveEvaluator.evaluate` and `MockFleet.evaluate`, so the golden eval samples exactly as production does (FR-5.1) and a test fails if one of them drifts. The judge's free-text candidate is still sampled greedily (TASK-FREE-TEXT-SIMILARITY changes that).

The live-run harness (`python -m ratchet.liverun`, [ADR 0121](adr/0121-live-measurement-method.md)) is tested the same way: `LiveRunWorld` builds a live `Runtime` on Token Factory and vLLM fakes with its ledger in a file, and the harness gets its seams as default arguments (`client=` an `httpx.Client` on `AppTransport(gateway.app)` that records every path and header, `provider_factory=` over the fakes, `run=` a `RecordingRun`, `http=`, `tavily=`, `clock=` a `FakeClock`, `timer=` and `sleep=`). The module fixture `liverun_world` runs one 3-round run with nights in process once and most of the tests read its run directory. Tests that need the gateway's seeded audit draws in a fixed order pass `--concurrency 1`.

These tests prove the wiring, the order of operations and the failure handling. They do not prove that the real services accept the requests: model ids, `nebius` CLI flags, vLLM's runtime LoRA loading and GPU training stay `untested (live)` in the PRD until the day-1 checks (`scripts/day1_check.py`) and a live run pass.

Found while writing them: without `RATCHET_STUDENT_BASE`, `NebiusTrainer` ran the sync and the paid training job before noticing there was no endpoint to load the adapter into. It now checks first, and `python -m ratchet.live reload` gives the same clear error instead of an httpx URL error (`test_live_night_checks_the_student_endpoint_before_paid_work` in `test_hardening.py`).

## Hot-path tests

`tests/test_perf.py` measures time, which a busy CI machine can stretch. Its timing tests stay stable by design: models answer with zero latency, so only Ratchet's own work is timed; each bound is a ratio between two runs on the same machine (365 versions against one) or a ceiling several times above what a laptop measures (a warm tick takes about 3 ms against 20, a request about 0.3 ms against 5); each figure is the best of three runs or a median; and the 200,000 rows go in with one `executemany`, so the whole file runs in about 3 s. The structural claims (which index a query searches, how many sorts a stats read does, that routing reads no version row) are checked exactly, with `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` and the connection's trace callback, so they do not depend on timing at all.

## Contract tests

`tests/test_contract.py` was written before the v0.5 rewrites of `Ratchet.handle` and `gateway.chat` (request validation and normalisation, field comparators), as their regression net. Both have landed (`tests/test_openai_compat.py`, `tests/test_tasks.py`), and their five strict xfails now pass as ordinary tests. Each test pins behaviour that someone outside the module depends on: an OpenAI client (status codes, `/v1/models`, task selection), the dashboard (`/ratchet/stats`), an auditor (`/ratchet/bom`, license fields), or the operator's bill (cost to the token).

Rules:

- **The stats keys only grow.** `tests/fixtures/stats_keys.json` lists every key the dashboard reads from `/ratchet/stats` (it mirrors `Stats` in `web/src/lib/api.ts`) with its JSON type; a `[]` key is a list whose every item is checked. A change may add keys to the payload and to the fixture. It must not remove or retype one, so the React dashboard and other clients keep working. The test also fails if the fixture loses one of the keys this contract started with.
- **The cost report keys only grow.** `tests/fixtures/cost_keys.json` does the same for `GET /ratchet/cost` and the dashboard's Economics panel (it mirrors `CostReport` in `web/src/lib/api.ts`), checked by `test_cost_report_contract`.
- **The task API keys only grow.** `tests/fixtures/tasks_keys.json` does the same for the task API and the dashboard's Task registry panel (it mirrors `TaskRegistry`, `TaskEntry`, `FieldDoc` and the write results in `web/src/lib/api.ts`), checked by `test_task_registry_contract`. The Resilience panel's event grammar is not a fixture: the test reads it from the panel's source, so the event text and the parser change together.
- **Known bugs are strict xfails that name the fixing item**, a v0.5 work item (none today: `API-VALIDATE-NORMALIZE` removed its four and `TASK-COMPARATORS` its one). When the fix lands the test passes, strict mode turns that into a failure, and the fix removes the marker in the same change. Write the mark literally (`@pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, reason=...)`, or `marks=pytest.mark.xfail(...)` in a `pytest.param`): the traceability scan reads marks from the source, so a mark held in a variable would make an xfail count as proof.
- Cost assertions are token-exact: each model gets distinct token counts, and the expected cost is built from `Settings.price(...)`, so a term billed with the wrong tokens, price or multiplicity fails.
- The dashboard's rendering is still checked by hand ([web/AGENTS.md](../web/AGENTS.md)); `test_stats_contract` proves the data it renders. FR-7.6 (light and dark mode, phone width) stays a manual check.

## Simulation methodology

`sim/simulate.py` runs `--days` days. Each day sends `--requests` requests from the chosen `--workload` through `Ratchet.handle()`, calls `ReleaseGate.tick()` ten times, then runs `run_night()`. It prints a day table and the event log, and writes JSON (`--out`, default `sim_results.json`).

| Flag | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `--days` | 8 | Days to simulate |
| `--requests` | 2000 | Requests per day |
| `--threshold` | 0.80 | Fallback threshold before calibration |
| `--audit-rate` | 0.10 | Higher than live (0.05) so canaries move in a few days |
| `--min-shadow` | 100 | `ReleasePolicy.min_shadow` |
| `--min-audits` | 15 | `ReleasePolicy.min_audits` (live default 20) |
| `--min-new` | 50 | `run_night(min_new_examples=…)` |
| `--drift-day` | 5 | Product launch day (0 = never) |
| `--bad-deploy-version` | 0 | Fault injection: this version is served corrupted once in canary/live |
| `--workload` | `tickets` | `tickets` (support tickets), `research` (Scout's triage and reader calls), `mixed` (half each), `schema` (structured outputs) or `tools` (support requests with the mock tool catalogue). With `tickets` the night trains on `ticket-triage` only; otherwise on every task |
| `--seed` | 7 | Seed for traffic and routing |
| `--out` | `sim_results.json` | Results file |

### Assumptions (these drive the numbers; they are not measurements)

**Traffic** (`sim/tickets.py`):

- 20 templates across 6 categories (billing, technical, account, shipping, refund, security) and 4 priorities. 6 of them are marked `hard`.
- Names, products and order numbers vary, so some templates produce many distinct texts and others very few (frequent repeats).
- From `--drift-day`, 20% of traffic comes from 3 new "Ratchet Watch" templates the student has never seen (1 of them hard).

**Research traffic** (`sim/research.py`, `--workload research`):

- 8 questions about well-known ML ideas, each phrased 4 ways, each with a pool of 72 simulated search results (12 templates × 6 variants). 60% of requests are `source-triage` calls, 40% `page-read` calls.
- Each template has fixed labels. 6 of the 12 are `hard` (for example a good forum answer next to an off-topic one, or a broad survey paper).
- From `--drift-day`, 20% of traffic comes from questions 7 and 8, which the student has not seen.
- `mixed` alternates between the ticket and research streams at random (50/50, its own seed), so both keep their own sequences.

**Schema and tools traffic** (`sim/generic.py`, `--workload schema`, `--workload tools`; [ADR 0041](adr/0041-generic-mock-world.md)):

- `schema`: requests with `response_format` json_schema over three schemas, each its own task (`review-rating`: an enum, a 1 to 5 integer, a boolean; `ticket-route`: two enums and a boolean; `meeting-extract`: two enums, an integer and an array of enums). From `--drift-day`, 20% of traffic is a fourth schema the student has not seen (`warranty-claim`, with nested records through `$ref`). Prompts combine products, opinions and a four-digit reference, so most texts are distinct.
- `tools`: support requests sent with the mock catalogue (`lookup_order`, `refund`) under `order-support`: 40% look-ups, 45% refunds with an amount and a reason, 15% questions no tool answers; new wordings from `--drift-day`.
- The right answer is drawn from sha256 of the request's text, not read from it (the mock teacher is right by definition). A schema's labels are its enum, boolean and number fields; a template is a schema name (or a tool set, or a system prompt), and a schema with more than three labels is `hard`.
- A tool request is answered by the teacher alone and never stored (FR-1.41), so the `tools` workload never trains the student: its share stays 0 and it saves nothing (until tool-call distillation).

**Mock teacher:**

- Always right on the labels.
- Bills `approx_tokens(prompt)` input plus answer and 300 reasoning output tokens.
- 2.4 s latency.

**Mock student skill** for a ticket of template `t`:

- If its exact text was in training: skill = 0.985.
- Otherwise: skill = max(0.35, CEIL × (1 − e^(−n/6))), where `n` is the number of distinct training examples of `t`, and CEIL = 0.985 (easy) or 0.86 (hard).
- Whether the student "knows" a ticket is fixed per (version, text).

**Sample behaviour:**

- **Knows:** each sample is right with probability 0.97; token probability 0.80–0.97.
- **Doesn't know, confidently wrong** (15% of unknown cases): all samples give the same wrong labels with probability 0.62–0.92. This deliberately overlaps with correct answers, so calibration cannot separate them perfectly. That is the case audits and shadow exist for.
- **Doesn't know, otherwise:** random labels, probability 0.35–0.65.

Research items use the same skill model, per result template. Scout's pinned planner and writer prompts are answered by the mock teacher only.

**Fault injection:**

- The corrupted version is fine on the staging copy, which golden eval and shadow use.
- Once it reaches canary or live, its "fragile" slices become confidently wrong: billing, refund and security tickets; `paper` and `docs` triage results; `date` reads; in the generic world refund calls, schema answers whose first enum takes its first value, and free text framed as steps. This models pushing the wrong adapter file to production.

**Costs:** Ultra $1/$3 per 1M tokens (the list price the simulator uses, see `ratchet/config.py`; research notes, 2026-10-25). The student uses the $0.06/$0.24 proxy, with 3 samples billed.

### Typical result (seed 7, 10 days, 2,000/day, drift day 5, bad deploy v3)

This run is the pinned reference ([below](#the-pinned-reference-simulation)), so these numbers cannot drift silently.

```
day  student  escal. audit ok     $/1k all-Ultra  saved
  1       0%      0%        -    1.093     1.093     0%
  2      44%      1%     100%    0.675     1.093    38%
  3      98%      2%     100%    0.160     1.093    85%
  4      98%      2%      93%    0.160     1.093    85%   v3 canary → rolled back (41% audited precision)
  5      86%     14%      97%    0.272     1.093    75%   product launch
  6      88%     12%      92%    0.246     1.093    77%
  7      88%     12%      97%    0.255     1.093    77%
  8      95%      5%      99%    0.200     1.093    82%
  9      98%      2%      97%    0.162     1.093    85%
 10      98%      2%      99%    0.147     1.093    87%
total saved 69%
```

### Seed robustness (10 days, 2,000/day, drift day 5, bad deploy v3)

| Seed | Final student share | Final-day savings | Total savings | Min daily audit agreement | Bad v3 outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 98% | 85% | 69% | 97% | rejected before deploy |
| 2 | 98% | 86% | 66% | 96% | rejected before deploy |
| 3 | 98% | 87% | 67% | 93% | rolled back |
| 11 | 98% | 85% | 66% | 92% | rolled back |

"Total" includes the cold-start days, when all traffic goes to the teacher.

### The drift alarm (10 days, 2,000/day, ticket workload, no fault injection)

Simulation, mock models ([ADR 0032](adr/0032-label-free-drift-alarm.md)). With the product launch on day 5, the drift monitor's first alarm, on the live version's `ticket-triage` stream; without it, none:

| Seed | Launch on day 5: first alarm | Requests into the launch day | Without the launch |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | day 5 (v1) | 137 | no alarm |
| 2 | day 5 (v2) | 246 | no alarm |
| 3 | day 5 (v1) | 70 | no alarm |
| 7 | day 5 (v1) | 379 | no alarm |
| 11 | day 5 (v1) | 437 | no alarm |
| 23 | day 5 (v1) | 190 | no alarm |

The reference run (seed 7, with the corrupted v3) alarms on v1 on day 5 as well; the corrupted v3 is no traffic change and raises none (its rollback is the audits' job). At 600 requests a day seeds 7 and 11 do not alarm within two days of the launch, and the mixed and research workloads (seed 7, 1,500 a day) raise none in 8 days: power depends on the volume per stream and on how far confidence falls.

The ticket numbers above are the same as in v0.1. Adding the research world did not change the random draws for tickets ([sim/AGENTS.md](../sim/AGENTS.md)).

### The teacher watch (10 days, 2,000/day, ticket workload, launch on day 5)

Simulation, mock models ([ADR 0033](adr/0033-teacher-watch.md)). `--teacher-noise 0.15 --teacher-noise-day 5`: from day 5 the mock teacher changes a ticket's category with probability 0.15. The freeze and what the epoch's checks saw by the end; without the noise, none:

| Seed | `teacher_drift` (Night School frozen from) | Checks: duplicates / probes | Disagreements | Strongest stretch | Without the noise |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | day 7 | 244 / 140 | 17 | 17 of 95 from day 5 | no freeze, 0 of 445 disagree |
| 2 | day 6 | 264 / 100 | 13 | 13 of 57 from day 5 | no freeze, 0 of 456 |
| 3 | day 6 | 256 / 120 | 17 | 17 of 74 from day 5 | no freeze, 0 of 456 |
| 7 | day 7 | 251 / 120 | 19 | 19 of 73 from day 5 | no freeze, 0 of 451 |
| 11 | day 6 | 269 / 120 | 16 | 16 of 74 from day 5 | no freeze, 0 of 463 |
| 23 | day 5 | 248 / 100 | 15 | 15 of 51 from day 5 | no freeze, 0 of 449 |

Most checks come free from duplicates, but after the first days the student serves most repeats, so the probes (20 a night) find about as many disagreements as the traffic does. With the noise off, a run with the watch on has the same days, versions and events as one with it off (`test_sim_a_stable_teacher_moves_no_number`), and the reference run and the three pinned workloads do not move.

### Workloads: research and mixed

Simulation, mock models. 8 days × 1,500 requests/day, drift day 5, no fault injection (selected days shown). Both workloads have several tasks on one adapter, so each task has its own threshold and its own golden check ([per-task calibration](adr/0040-per-task-calibration.md), on by default):

```
python -m sim.simulate --workload research --days 8 --requests 1500     (seed 7)
day  student  escal. audit ok     $/1k all-Ultra  saved
  1       0%      0%        -    1.133     1.133     0%
  2      15%      3%     100%    0.987     1.131    13%
  3      94%      6%      99%    0.205     1.132    82%
  4      93%      7%      99%    0.218     1.132    81%
  5      93%      7%      98%    0.197     1.132    83%
  8      92%      8%      98%    0.211     1.132    81%
total: 12000 requests, $5.11 vs $13.58 all-Ultra -> saved 62%

python -m sim.simulate --workload mixed --days 8 --requests 1500        (seed 7)
day  student  escal. audit ok     $/1k all-Ultra  saved
  1       0%      0%        -    1.112     1.112     0%
  3      19%      2%     100%    0.932     1.112    16%
  4      92%      8%      98%    0.226     1.111    80%
  5      86%     14%      96%    0.265     1.111    76%
  8      94%      6%      97%    0.202     1.112    82%
total: 12000 requests, $6.48 vs $13.34 all-Ultra -> saved 51%
```

Seeds 1, 2, 3, 7, 11 (same settings):

| Workload | Final student share | Final-day savings | Total savings | Lowest daily audit agreement | First version live |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| research | 65–97% | 56–86% | 19–65% | 97–98% | day 2–6 |
| mixed | 90–96% | 78–85% | 47–60% | 96–98% | day 2–3 |
| research, `RATCHET_PER_TASK_CALIBRATION=0` | 96–98% | 85–86% | 57–65% | 96–98% | day 2–3 |
| mixed, `RATCHET_PER_TASK_CALIBRATION=0` | 92–98% | 79–86% | 54–63% | 93–96% | day 2–3 |

On mixed traffic the first student comes a night later with seed 7 (v1 was rejected at 94.3% golden precision), so total savings are lower. With one pooled threshold and the pooled gate (`RATCHET_PER_TASK_CALIBRATION=0`, v0.4's behaviour) the student serves more at a lower worst day of audited agreement. Per task, each task's threshold and its golden check rest on a few dozen held-out rows, and the check is a point estimate, as the global gate's is: a good task dips under its 95% floor, or more than 2 points under live's 100% on it, now and then, and is disabled on that version (research, seed 11: the first version's `page-read` at 18 of 19 right; its canary, answering one task at 10% of traffic, then took until day 6 to collect its audits). A version with a disabled task that live still answers usually loses the shadow's coverage comparison too, so it is rejected a day later (research, seed 1, nights 3 to 5).

### Workloads: schema and tools

Simulation, mock models. 8 days × 1,500 requests/day, drift day 5, no fault injection. Both come from the generic mock world ([ADR 0041](adr/0041-generic-mock-world.md)); their right answers are drawn from each request's text, so these numbers show the loop on structured outputs, not how well a model reads a review.

```
python -m sim.simulate --workload schema --days 8 --requests 1500       (seed 7)
day  student  escal. audit ok     $/1k all-Ultra  saved
  1       0%      0%        -    1.020     1.020     0%
  2      15%      4%     100%    0.893     1.020    12%
  3      77%     19%     100%    0.334     1.020    67%
  4      85%     15%      97%    0.248     1.020    76%
  5      80%     20%      96%    0.312     1.023    70%
  8      93%      7%      97%    0.193     1.023    81%
total: 12000 requests, $5.33 vs $12.26 all-Ultra -> saved 57%

python -m sim.simulate --workload tools --days 8 --requests 1500        (seed 7)
day  student  escal. audit ok     $/1k all-Ultra  saved
  1       0%      0%        -    1.068     1.068     0%
  2       0%      0%        -    1.069     1.069     0%
  8       0%      0%        -    1.069     1.069     0%
total: 12000 requests, $12.83 vs $12.83 all-Ultra -> saved 0%
```

Seeds 1, 2, 3, 7, 11 (same settings):

| Workload | Final student share | Final-day savings | Total savings | Lowest daily audit agreement | First version live |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| schema | 70–93% | 60–81% | 40–57% | 92–97% | day 3 |
| tools | 0% | 0% | 0% | no audits | never (nothing stored) |
| schema, `RATCHET_PER_TASK_CALIBRATION=0` | 86–94% | 75–82% | 55–61% | 92–96% | day 2–3 |

On `schema` the drift schema (`warranty-claim`, five or more labels, `hard`) holds the share near 80% for two nights from day 5. Its three tasks share one adapter, so per-task calibration (on by default) applies, and with some seeds a task disabled on a few dozen held-out rows lowers the share, as on the research workload above; `tools` has one task and is the same either way. On `tools` every request offers tools, so the mock teacher answers it alone with its tool calls and nothing is stored (FR-1.41, [ADR 0043](adr/0043-tool-calls-teacher-only-by-default.md)): no night trains, the student never serves and the run saves nothing. It shows what the gateway does with tool requests until tool-call distillation; before FR-1.41 both models answered these prompts in prose with the tools stripped, and that prose was learned within two nights.

## Bugs the simulation found (and the fixes)

Running the loop end to end exposed design flaws that unit tests would not have caught. Each fix is in the code now.

| # | Symptom in the simulation | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Every new candidate rejected as "regresses vs live" for days | Candidate measured on today's golden set, live measured on its own (older, easier) golden set from the day it was trained | **Champion vs challenger:** `run_night` re-evaluates the live version on the same golden set first and overwrites its eval (`rechecked_day`) |
| 2 | Every candidate rejected in shadow at ~70% "agreement" | Shadow judged on all traffic, including requests it would have escalated; and an agreement with the live student was the only reference | Shadow runs the full cascade: records `shadow_serve` and judges **precision only on what it would serve**, plus **coverage** vs live. Disagreements with the live student are **arbitrated by the teacher** |
| 3 | Student never learned the most common questions | The hash-based golden split held some frequently repeated requests out of training forever | Requests seen at least `FREQUENT = 3` times always go to training and leave the golden set |
| 4 | Canary never finished; promotions stalled | Each night's new candidate replaced the version in canary | `run_night` **skips training while a shadow or canary is in flight**, which also saves GPU time |
| 5 | Threshold promised 95% precision but delivered 94% | `calibrate_threshold` checked precision before including all points tied at the same confidence | Walk down in groups of equal confidence; return `inf` (serve nothing) if no threshold qualifies; `cascade_eval` caps it at 1.01 |
| 6 | Golden coverage 94% vs real coverage 80% | Deduplication meant the golden set over-represented rare phrasings, and the threshold was calibrated and measured on the same rows | `seen` count stored per example; **calibrate on one half of the golden set, measure on the other**; frequent requests handled by fix 3 |

Fixes 1, 3, 4 and 6 also have direct tests now: `test_champion_rescored_on_same_golden_set`, `test_golden_rows_never_reach_the_trainer_except_frequent`, `test_night_skips_with_reason` and `test_cascade_eval_measures_on_held_out_half`; fix 2 is covered by `test_shadow_promotes_and_both_rejections` and `test_shadow_arbitration_calls_teacher_once_and_stores_example`.

Two more problems were fixed earlier in development. A fixed global threshold let about 10% wrong answers through, which led to per-version calibration ([ADR 0003](adr/0003-per-request-cascade-with-calibrated-confidence.md)). A point-estimate rollback rule would have rolled back healthy canaries about 19% of the time at n=20, which led to the Wilson upper bound ([ADR 0005](adr/0005-shadow-canary-rollback-with-wilson-bounds.md)).

## Issues found while documenting, and their fixes

Writing these docs turned up ten gaps in v0.1. Each one is fixed in v0.1.1 and covered by `tests/test_hardening.py`.

| # | Issue | Fix | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Live gateway never advanced `Ledger.day`, so every request landed in one bucket | `Ledger(auto_day=True)` in live mode: day 1 starts at the first logged request, one day per 24 hours | `test_auto_day_counts_days_from_first_request` |
| 2 | A student HTTP error became a 500 | Student errors fall back to the teacher and log a `student_error` event (1st, 10th, 100th, 1000th). A teacher error returns **502** | `test_student_failure_falls_back_to_teacher`, `test_teacher_failure_returns_502` |
| 3 | Runtime-loaded LoRA adapters are lost when the endpoint restarts | New command `python -m ratchet.live reload` re-loads live, canary and shadow adapters | `test_reload_adapters_loads_live_canary_shadow` (in `test_live.py`, against a fake vLLM); on a real endpoint, see [RUNBOOK.md](RUNBOOK.md) |
| 4 | The untuned fallback model was judged with the tuned version's threshold | `Ratchet.threshold(version, model)` uses the global default for `RATCHET_FALLBACK_STUDENT` | `test_fallback_model_uses_default_threshold` |
| 5 | `alert` events repeated on every tick | One alert per live version | `test_live_alert_fires_once_per_version` |
| 6 | No schema migration for existing database files | `Ledger._migrate()` adds missing columns on open (`MIGRATIONS` table in `ledger.py`) | `test_old_database_file_is_migrated` |
| 7 | Stale docstring and column comment | Fixed | n/a |
| 8 | Unknown `X-Ratchet-Task` values were stored forever in `tasks.REGISTRY` | Unknown names get a temporary whole-text task; names must match `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]{1,64}` | `test_unknown_tasks_do_not_grow_registry` |
| 9 | `ReleasePolicy` could only be changed in code | `ReleasePolicy.from_env()` reads `RATCHET_<FIELD>` (e.g. `RATCHET_MIN_AUDITS`) | `test_policy_from_env` |
| 10 | LoRA targets might miss Nemotron-H Mamba layers | `train.py --targets` (default attention and MLP, `TARGETS_ATTENTION_MLP`, as the measured adapters; `TARGETS_WITH_MAMBA` adds `in_proj,out_proj`, to confirm on day 1) | The default: `test_train_parse_args_defaults_and_row_contract`; the module names: manual (GPU) |

Still open: `RATCHET_SYNC_CMD` and `RATCHET_TRAIN_CMD` run without a shell, so pipes and `&&` do not work; wrap them in a script.

## The pinned reference simulation

`tests/fixtures/sim_reference_seed7.json` holds one run of the simulator with every input pinned (seed 7, 10 days × 2,000 requests, drift day 5, corrupted v3, mock models; the same run as `make sim`): the inputs, each day's requests, student share, escalation rate, savings and audited agreement rounded to 4 decimal places, the ordered Night School and release events as (day, kind, version), and each version's final state, traffic and training-set size. `test_reference_simulation_unchanged` runs it once per session (the session-scoped `reference_run` fixture, about 7 s) and compares exactly. The inputs compared are the ones the fixture pins: a simulator flag added later, whose default keeps the old behaviour, does not force a regeneration, while a changed or removed pinned input fails the test.

It and the reference workloads below are the deliberate exceptions to "assert on trends, not numbers". Refactors and new features that claim to leave the legacy behaviour untouched prove it by keeping this test green.
